        Idea:
Migrate the Python worker from pip/venv to uv with a lockfile, running `uv sync` unconditionally on every launch. Scope spans THREE components: (1) the Python worker repo (add pyproject.toml + uv.lock from existing requirements.txt; no code changes inside the worker), (2) the client's Settings modal where install/run command strings live (likely commandUtils.ts), and (3) the orchestrator that spawns workers using those same command strings. Key questions the plan must answer: where the install/run command strings are actually defined and whether client and orchestrator share that code or duplicate it; how `uv` itself gets onto end-user machines (bundle / detect-and-download / one-time install instruction); migration path for existing users with stale pre-uv .venv directories; cross-platform behavior on Linux/macOS/Windows; lockfile generation matching current resolved versions; rollback story if `uv sync` fails (network down, corrupted lockfile); and the Settings modal UX after migration (does the manual install button become a 'force resync' button?). Deliverables: (1) every file to touch across worker/client/orchestrator, (2) decision on uv bootstrapping with justification, (3) migration path for existing users, (4) test plan covering Linux/macOS/Windows + fresh-install + upgrade-from-pip-venv, (5) rollback plan.

        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: Migrate Worker Bootstrap From pip/venv to uv

## Overview
The worker repo already has a minimal [pyproject.toml](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker/pyproject.toml), but it is not a real dependency source of truth yet; the actual runtime install still comes from loose requirements files and pip/venv. The client’s local-worker commands are generated in [commandUtils.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/shared/components/SettingsModal/commandUtils.ts). The live orchestrator path still imports the legacy [runpod_client.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker-orchestrator/gpu_orchestrator/runpod_client.py) from [control_loop.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker-orchestrator/gpu_orchestrator/control_loop.py), even though a newer template-backed startup path already exists under [gpu_orchestrator/runpod/](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker-orchestrator/gpu_orchestrator/runpod).

That means the client and orchestrator do not share install/run command code today, and the orchestrator is internally duplicated as well. The simplest fundamental fix is to converge the orchestrator onto the template-backed `gpu_orchestrator.runpod` path first, then migrate one startup script to `uv` instead of editing two parallel implementations.

## Decision: UV Bootstrapping
Use detect-and-download via the official Astral installer in both the client-generated commands and the RunPod startup script.

Justification:
- Bundling `uv` is not realistic here because the client is a browser UI that only shows copyable shell commands, not a local binary/agent.
- One-time manual instructions are worse UX than embedding the bootstrap directly into the install command.
- The vendored Wan2GP installer already follows this pattern, so it matches repository precedent.
- The official installer avoids depending on an already-working Python/pip toolchain just to install `uv`.

Linux and RunPod should use the official shell installer and export `~/.local/bin` into `PATH`. Windows should invoke the official PowerShell installer even when the selected shell is `cmd`, then refresh `PATH` before `uv sync`.

## Phase 1: Converge on One Runtime Path

### Step 1: Cut the live orchestrator over to the template-backed RunPod package
**Scope:** Medium
1. Switch active callers from legacy `gpu_orchestrator.runpod_client` imports to `gpu_orchestrator.runpod` so the worker spawn path goes through the existing template-backed startup flow.
2. Leave the legacy `runpod_client.py` in place as a compatibility module for now, but stop using it from the control loop and operational scripts.
3. Confirm API parity first with cheap checks so this import cutover lands before any `uv` behavior changes.

### Step 2: Turn the worker repo into a real uv project with a lockfile
**Scope:** Large
1. Expand [pyproject.toml](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker/pyproject.toml) into a real PEP 621 project while preserving the existing pytest config and entrypoints.
2. Translate the current dependency graph from `requirements.txt` plus `Wan2GP/requirements.txt` into `pyproject.toml` dependencies.
3. Model the current torch split as explicit extras or dependency groups, for example `cuda124` and `cuda128`, so one committed `uv.lock` can represent both the client’s GPU selector and the orchestrator’s fixed CUDA 12.4 worker image.
4. Generate [uv.lock](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker/uv.lock) from clean throwaway installs of the current pip workflow, then compare the resulting pinned graph to `uv export --locked` for each supported extra. If they differ, pin the drifting ranges in `pyproject.toml` until the lock matches current behavior instead of silently upgrading packages.

### Step 3: Standardize the runtime contract around `uv sync` plus `uv run`
**Scope:** Medium
1. Make the runtime contract be: repo present, `uv` present, `uv sync --locked --extra <gpu-profile>` on every launch, then `uv run python run_worker.py ...`.
2. Avoid shell activation entirely. This removes the current `venv/bin/activate` and `venv\\Scripts\\activate` branches and makes Linux/Windows behavior more uniform.
3. Keep `requirements.txt` in the repo during rollout as rollback ballast, but stop using it as a runtime install source.

## Phase 2: Update User-Facing and Runtime Command Surfaces

### Step 4: Rewrite the Settings modal commands and keep the UX simple
**Scope:** Medium
1. Update [commandUtils.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/shared/components/SettingsModal/commandUtils.ts) so first-time install commands do all of: clone repo, install `uv` if missing, perform one-time env backup if this is the first uv-managed launch, run `uv sync --locked --extra cuda124|cuda128`, then start the worker with `uv run python run_worker.py ...`.
2. Update run commands so they always do `git pull`, unconditional `uv sync --locked --extra ...`, then `uv run python run_worker.py ...`.
3. Keep the two-tab model rather than turning Install into a dedicated force-resync button. The `Run` tab already becomes “pull + resync + launch” every time, while `Install` remains the first-time bootstrap and repair path.
4. Update [GenerationTokenPanel.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/shared/components/SettingsModal/sections/GenerationSection/components/GenerationTokenPanel.tsx) copy so users understand that every normal launch now resyncs dependencies automatically.
5. Update [commandUtils.test.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/shared/components/SettingsModal/commandUtils.test.ts) and [GenerationTokenPanel.test.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/shared/components/SettingsModal/sections/GenerationSection/components/GenerationTokenPanel.test.tsx) first, before broader UI work.

### Step 5: Rewrite the orchestrator startup script to match the same contract
**Scope:** Medium
1. Replace the pip/venv bootstrap in [worker_startup.template.sh](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker-orchestrator/gpu_orchestrator/runpod/worker_startup.template.sh) with the same pattern: install `uv` if missing, migrate old envs once, `git pull`, `uv sync --locked --extra cuda124`, then `uv run python run_worker.py ...`.
2. Remove the current hash-based pip repair logic from the active startup path because unconditional `uv sync` makes that logic redundant and risky to maintain in parallel.
3. Keep failure logging explicit: the startup script should stop on `uv sync` failure, log the exact failing step, and let `check_worker_startup_status` surface that state instead of silently starting with a partially updated env.
4. Update orchestrator tests to assert that rendered startup scripts contain `uv` bootstrap, unconditional `uv sync --locked`, and the `uv run` launch line.

### Step 6: Update owned docs and debug helpers that still teach pip/venv
**Scope:** Small
1. Update the worker quick-start in [README.md](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker/README.md) to the new `uv` flow.
2. Update [pod.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/scripts/debug/commands/pod.py) so support/debug output matches the real runtime commands.
3. Update [task_worker_lifecycle.md](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/docs/structure_detail/task_worker_lifecycle.md) so internal architecture docs stop pointing people at stale pip/venv instructions.
4. Do not try to mass-update the vendored Wan2GP upstream docs in this change; keep the scope to Reigh-owned surfaces.

## Phase 3: Migration, Validation, and Rollout Safety

### Step 7: Implement the one-time migration path for existing users
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add a repo-local migration sentinel written by the launch scripts only after the first successful uv-managed sync.
2. On the first uv-managed launch, if the sentinel is absent, move any existing `venv/` or `.venv/` to timestamped backup names such as `venv.pre-uv-<timestamp>` or `.venv.pre-uv-<timestamp>` before running `uv sync`.
3. If the first `uv sync` fails, do not write the sentinel. That preserves the backup env and makes recovery straightforward.
4. After the sentinel exists, subsequent launches just run `uv sync --locked` unconditionally and use the managed `.venv`.

## Migration Path
- Fresh install: clone repo, bootstrap `uv`, run `uv sync --locked --extra <gpu-profile>`, then `uv run python run_worker.py ...`.
- Upgrade from current local pip/venv install: first uv-managed launch backs up `venv/` and `.venv/`, then creates the uv-managed environment and writes the migration sentinel only on success.
- Upgrade on RunPod: the same one-time backup behavior runs inside `/workspace/<repo>`, then the pod continues with the uv-managed env.
- macOS: keep local worker unsupported in the modal and continue steering macOS users to cloud processing rather than emitting a misleading local `uv` bootstrap flow.

## Test Plan
- Linux local fresh install, `cuda124`, no existing env.
- Windows PowerShell fresh install, `cuda124`, no existing env.
- Windows PowerShell fresh install, `cuda128`, no existing env.
- Linux local upgrade from existing `venv/`.
- Windows local upgrade from existing `venv/`.
- Linux local upgrade from existing `.venv/`.
- RunPod/Linux orchestrator upgrade from existing `venv/` on the mounted workspace.
- macOS Settings modal smoke test: unsupported message still shown and no local worker install path is presented.
- Failure path: `uv` missing but installer available.
- Failure path: network down during `uv sync` after a prior successful migration.
- Failure path: intentionally corrupted `uv.lock` stops startup and produces actionable logs.

### Step 8: Define the rollback plan and ship it with the change
**Scope:** Small
1. Runtime rollback on first migration: because the old env is moved to a timestamped backup before the first uv-managed sync, support can restore the old environment by renaming the backup back into place if the first migration fails badly.
2. Runtime failure after migration: do not add a pip fallback path. If `uv sync` fails, fail the launch loudly and keep the last committed lockfile plus logs as the debugging surface. Mixing two package managers would recreate the same duplication this change is trying to remove.
3. Release rollback: keep `requirements.txt` in the repo during rollout so reverting the client command strings, orchestrator template, and worker `pyproject.toml`/`uv.lock` is a straightforward code rollback if the migration needs to be backed out.

## Execution Order
1. Cut the orchestrator over to `gpu_orchestrator.runpod` first so only one active startup implementation remains.
2. Land the worker `pyproject.toml` and committed `uv.lock` next so every downstream command has a real target.
3. Update client command builders and orchestrator startup script in the same release window.
4. Update docs/debug surfaces after the runtime paths are correct.

## Validation Order
1. Start with the cheapest checks: worker lock generation, client command-string tests, orchestrator startup-script render tests.
2. Run fresh-install smoke tests next.
3. Run upgrade-from-pip/venv smoke tests after that.
4. Finish with explicit failure-path tests for missing `uv`, offline sync, and corrupted lockfile.

  {
    "subsystem": "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct",
    "total_occurrences": 3,
    "plan_count": 1,
    "entries": [
      {
        "id": "DEBT-007",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: for ordinary pairs, the new diagnosis is not well-supported by the repo. the current sync effect in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l62) already syncs `url` and `primaryvariantid`, and the regenerate path reads exactly `starturl`, `endurl`, `startgenid`, `endgenid`, `startvariantid`, and `endvariantid` from `segmentslotmode.pairdata` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts#l438). adding `thumburl` and `generationid` to the sync does not, by itself, explain why an old image url is still being used for regular pairs.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-008",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the fix is incomplete for extra trailing slots. `handlepairclick` can populate `activepairdata` from `trailingpairdata` when `pairindex === pairdatabyindex.size` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l121), but the proposed sync effect only reads `pairdatabyindex.get(segmentslotlightboxindex)`. for those trailing-slot cases `fresh` is `undefined`, so no image refresh happens.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-018",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the merge pseudocode does not actually sync 'all image fields' as claimed. it omits `id` and `position` from change detection, and when `fresh.startimage` or `fresh.endimage` becomes `null` it preserves the stale previous object instead of syncing that null state. as written, it also needs explicit null-guards around spreads of `prev.startimage` / `prev.endimage` to be safe in strict typescript.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      }
    ]

The UI still has explicit `Install` and `Run` tabs and no dedicated repair/resync button, so the plan's decision to keep the two-tab model matches the repo's existing UX seam rather than inventing a new control surface.","flagged":false},{"detail":"Checked the requested deliverable of enumerating every file to touch. The live `runpod_client.py` startup script writes `metadata.startup_phase` values such as `deps_installing`, `deps_verified`, `worker_starting`, and `ready`, and `gpu_orchestrator/worker_state.py` explicitly uses those values to suppress normal timeout logic; the template-backed `worker_startup.template.sh` has no equivalent phase updates today, so cutting over first would currently remove a real orchestrator invariant.","flagged":true},{"detail":"The lockfile design is under-specified against the actual runtime matrix in the repo. Local install commands hard-code Python 3.10 on Linux and accept Windows Python 3.10+, the RunPod worker image defaults to Python 3.11, and `reigh-worker/Wan2GP/requirements.txt` contains python-version-specific and platform-specific packages like `onnxruntime-gpu` and `insightface` wheels, so the plan cannot really claim version-faithful lock generation until it first fixes which Python minors are in scope.","flagged":true},{"detail":"Switching the orchestrator launch line from `python worker.py` to `uv run python run_worker.py` is a substantive process-model change, not just a package-manager swap. `run_worker.py` enters `source/runtime/supervisor.py`, which restarts on idle-release exits and crash loops, while the current RunPod scripts launch `worker.py` directly; the plan does not call out that behavioral change or add explicit validation for PID, restart, and logging expectations around it.","flagged":true}]},{"id":"scope","question":"Search for related code that handles the same concept. Is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?","findings":[{"detail":"Searches for Reigh-owned worker bootstrap guidance show the surface is broader than the plan's listed docs/helpers. In addition to `README.md`, `pod.py`, and one lifecycle doc, the repo still documents `worker.py` and legacy startup assumptions in `reigh-worker/debug/README.md`, `reigh-worker-orchestrator/scripts/debug/README.md`, `reigh-worker/STRUCTURE.md`, and `reigh-worker/docs/KIJAI_SVI_IMPLEMENTATION.md`, so the stale-guidance problem is wider than the current checklist.","flagged":true},{"detail":"Searches inside the vendored `Wan2GP` subtree show it already has its own `uv`-aware install/update tooling in `scripts/install.sh`, `scripts/install.bat`, and `setup.py`. Even if the rollout intentionally avoids editing vendored upstream files, the repo will still contain two different uv bootstrap stories unless the plan explicitly states which path is canonical for Reigh users and support.","flagged":true}]},{"id":"all_locations","question":"Does the change touch all locations AND supporting infrastructure?","findings":[{"detail":"Grepping `runpod_client` imports shows the legacy module is still referenced by more than the files named in the plan: besides `control_loop.py` and the listed scripts, it is also referenced from `reigh-worker/debug/commands/storage.py` and both logging allowlists in `gpu_orchestrator/logging_config.py` and `api_orchestrator/logging_config.py`. If the goal is a single active startup path and a true import cutover, those supporting locations are part of the same migration surface.","flagged":true},{"detail":"Supporting tests for the startup-phase contract are missing from the file list. `tests/gpu_orchestrator/control/phases/test_state.py` and `tests/gpu_orchestrator/control/phases/test_periodic.py` both encode behavior around `startup_phase` workers, so any cutover that changes startup signaling should touch those tests as well, not only the RunPod startup-script renderer tests.","flagged":true},{"detail":"The documentation duplication is also a supporting-infrastructure issue, not just a docs-polish issue. That assumption is exactly what failed in the original support transcript that motivated this whole migration: a Windows user opened cmd.exe in C:\Users\MC, copy-pasted 'pip install -r requirements.txt', and got 'No such file or directory' because requirements.txt wasn't in their cwd. After this migration, the same user pasting the new uv command from the same cwd would get 'error: No pyproject.toml found in current directory or any parent directory'. Same bug, one layer deeper. The plan currently fixes Windows PATH propagation (good — explicit %USERPROFILE%\.local\bin\uv.exe path) but does NOT fix the cwd problem, which is the actual transcript failure.

        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: Migrate Worker Bootstrap From pip/venv to uv

## Overview
Migrate the Python worker runtime from pip/venv to `uv` with a committed lockfile, and run `uv sync --locked` on every launch across the client Settings modal, the orchestrator RunPod startup template, and the worker repo itself.

Current shape verified in the repo:
- [reigh-worker/pyproject.toml](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker/pyproject.toml) exists but is not the runtime source of truth; installs come from [requirements.txt](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker/requirements.txt) and the vendored [Wan2GP/requirements.txt](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker/Wan2GP/requirements.txt).
- Client commands are built in [commandUtils.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/shared/components/SettingsModal/commandUtils.ts); Linux hard-codes `python3.10` (line 68); the launch line already uses `run_worker.py` (line 29).
- Both the live [runpod_client.py:1336](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker-orchestrator/gpu_orchestrator/runpod_client.py) and the template-backed [worker_startup.template.sh:328](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker-orchestrator/gpu_orchestrator/runpod/worker_startup.template.sh) launch `python worker.py` directly — not `run_worker.py`.
- **RunPod actually runs Python 3.10.** Base image is `runpod/pytorch:2.4.0-py3.11-...` ([runpod_client.py:357](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker-orchestrator/gpu_orchestrator/runpod_client.py)), but both `runpod_client.py:985/993` and `worker_startup.template.sh:105/113` apt-install `python3.10-venv` and explicitly create the runtime venv with `python3.10 -m venv`. Runtime interpreter is 3.10 everywhere.
- [runpod_client.py:890-922](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker-orchestrator/gpu_orchestrator/runpod_client.py) defines `update_worker_phase`, which writes `metadata.startup_phase` via Supabase REST but **silently swallows all errors** with `|| return 0` at lines 897, 903 and `|| true` at lines 912, 920.
- [worker_state.py:140](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker-orchestrator/gpu_orchestrator/worker_state.py) reads `metadata.get('startup_phase')` from an in-memory dict populated by Supabase polling. **There is NO pod-file reader anywhere in the orchestrator control loop** — confirmed by grep. The orchestrator cannot observe anything on the pod filesystem without SSH-exec, which is not on the hot path and out of scope here.
- `getInstallationCommand`/`getRunCommand` are referenced in exactly four files: `commandUtils.ts`, `commandUtils.test.ts`, `GenerationTokenPanel.tsx`, `GenerationTokenPanel.test.tsx`. `GenerationTokenPanel` is the only non-test caller and already guards `computerType === "mac"`.
- Legacy `runpod_client` references also live in [scripts/debug/commands/storage.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker-orchestrator/scripts/debug/commands/storage.py), [gpu_orchestrator/logging_config.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker-orchestrator/gpu_orchestrator/logging_config.py), [api_orchestrator/logging_config.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker-orchestrator/api_orchestrator/logging_config.py).
- `task_worker_lifecycle.md` is duplicated under both [docs/structure_detail/](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/docs/structure_detail/task_worker_lifecycle.md) and [reigh-app/docs/structure_detail/](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/docs/structure_detail/task_worker_lifecycle.md).

### Scope guardrails (pinned decisions)
- **Python matrix:** Linux local = 3.10, Windows local = 3.10, RunPod = 3.10. `pyproject.toml` declares `requires-python = ">=3.10,<3.11"`. **Every `uv sync`, `uv run`, and `uv lock` invocation pins `--python 3.10` explicitly** so uv cannot silently pick up the RunPod base image's `python3.11`.
- **Linux distro scope:** "Linux local" supported on distros that ship `python3.10-venv` as a native apt package (Ubuntu 22.04 LTS, Debian 12). Ubuntu 24.04+ does not package `python3.10-venv` by default; users on those distros need the deadsnakes PPA. This rollout does NOT embed a deadsnakes bootstrap in the generated install command (it would require user-specific sudo PPA actions); instead, `README.md` and the Settings modal help copy name Ubuntu 22.04/Debian 12 as the supported baseline and point Ubuntu 24.04+ users at deadsnakes as a manual prerequisite. Documented scope, not new code.
- **Interpreter management:** Keep `apt-get install python3.10-venv` as the Linux/RunPod prerequisite. Considered and rejected: `uv python install 3.10` (uv-managed toolchain) — it would land in `~/.local/share/uv/python`, changing the image's system interpreter layout, and would link Wan2GP C-extension wheels against different system libraries than the apt-installed interpreter does. Revisit post-rollout.
- **Process model:** orchestrator template keeps `python worker.py` as the launch line (wrapped as `uv run --python 3.10 python worker.py ...`). It does **not** switch to `run_worker.py` (which would route through [supervisor.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker/source/runtime/supervisor.py) — out of scope).
- **Canonical bootstrap:** Reigh-owned `reigh-worker/pyproject.toml` + `uv.lock` is the single source of truth. Vendored `Wan2GP/scripts/install.*` and `setup.py` are out of scope. **Accepted debt:** folding `Wan2GP/requirements.txt` into Reigh's `pyproject.toml` creates manual re-translation risk on future subtree pulls; documented in `reigh-worker/STRUCTURE.md` (Step 12). No CI diff check added.
- **macOS contract:** `getInstallationCommand`/`getRunCommand` throw `UnsupportedPlatformError` for mac. Verified safe because `GenerationTokenPanel` (only non-test caller) already guards with `computerType === "mac"`. **Accepted debt:** a `NonMacComputerType` narrow TypeScript type would make future regressions compile-time impossible; deferred as a follow-up, not in this rollout.
- **Phase signaling correction (vs. prior draft):** an earlier draft proposed a pod-local sentinel file + a `worker_state.py` fallback reader. That was wrong — there is no pod-file reader in the orchestrator and building one via SSH-exec on the polling hot path is out of scope and fragile during early startup before SSH keys are provisioned. **Correction:** hardening is done entirely on the pod side via a retry-then-exit on the first `deps_installing` PATCH. The orchestrator control loop (`worker_state.py`, `control/phases/periodic.py`) is NOT touched.

## Phase 1: Preserve Startup-Phase Invariant In the Template Path

### Step 1: Document the startup_phase contract (`gpu_orchestrator/runpod_client.py`, `gpu_orchestrator/worker_state.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Enumerate** every `metadata.startup_phase` value the live orchestrator writes and every place it's read across `runpod_client.py:890-922` and `worker_state.py:140,161,201`. Confirm `control/phases/periodic.py` reads the same metadata dict (no separate path).
2. **Audit** `update_worker_phase` at `runpod_client.py:890-922`: catalogue the four `|| return 0` / `|| true` swallow points so the template port knows which to preserve and which to harden.

### Step 2: Port startup_phase writes into the template with retry-then-exit on first PATCH (`gpu_orchestrator/runpod/worker_startup.template.sh`, `gpu_orchestrator/runpod/startup_script.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Port** the `update_worker_phase` shell helper from `runpod_client.py` into `worker_startup.template.sh` at identical transition points. Keep the existing pip/venv install steps intact in this step — only phase signaling is added. No uv changes yet.
2. **Harden the FIRST `deps_installing` PATCH only** with a bounded retry + hard exit:
   ```sh
   for attempt in 1 2 3; do
       if update_worker_phase_strict "deps_installing"; then
           break
       fi
       if [ "$attempt" = "3" ]; then
           echo "❌ FATAL: could not signal deps_installing to Supabase after 3 attempts; aborting so RunPod console surfaces the failure" >&2
           exit 1
       fi
       sleep 2
   done
   ```
   `update_worker_phase_strict` is a non-swallowing variant of the existing helper: `curl ... || return 1` instead of `|| return 0`, and the outer PATCH returns non-zero on HTTP error. It is used **only for this first transition**. Subsequent phase writes (`deps_verified`, `worker_starting`, `ready`) retain the legacy lenient semantics — they indicate progress but their absence is not fatal because the pod has by then already proven it can reach Supabase at least once.
3. **Rationale:** If the first PATCH fails permanently, the pod exits before running `uv sync`. `worker_state.py` then sees a worker that simply never set `startup_phase` (same as today for pods that die before signaling) and its 30-minute startup safety net at `worker_state.py:200-204` still applies; the difference is that the pod is **visibly terminated** in the RunPod console instead of silently marching into a broken `uv sync` with no phase signal at all. No orchestrator-side change is required.
4. **Parameterize** the phase-update target in `startup_script.py` if the rendered template needs an endpoint/token.

### Step 3: Template-side parity tests only (`tests/gpu_orchestrator/runpod/test_startup_script.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Extend** `test_startup_script.py` to assert the rendered template: (a) writes each of `deps_installing`, `deps_verified`, `worker_starting`, `ready` at the correct sequence points; (b) the first `deps_installing` branch uses the strict helper + retry loop + `exit 1` on persistent failure (not `|| true`); (c) subsequent phase writes remain lenient.
2. **Do NOT modify** `tests/gpu_orchestrator/control/phases/test_state.py` or `test_periodic.py`. The orchestrator control loop is untouched, so those tests do not need new coverage.

### Step 4: Cut active callers over to `gpu_orchestrator.runpod` (`gpu_orchestrator/control_loop.py`, `scripts/spawn_gpu.py`, `scripts/shutdown_all_workers.py`, `scripts/ssh_to_worker.py`, `scripts/terminate_single_worker.py`, `scripts/debug/services/workers.py`, `scripts/debug/commands/storage.py`, `gpu_orchestrator/logging_config.py`, `api_orchestrator/logging_config.py`, `tests/test_runpod_decomposition.py`, `tests/scripts/test_debug_services.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Switch** all live imports from `gpu_orchestrator.runpod_client` to `gpu_orchestrator.runpod`.
2. **Update** both logging allowlists so the new module name is whitelisted and the old one is removed.
3. **Leave** `runpod_client.py` as an unreferenced compatibility shim; actual removal is a follow-up.
4. **Run** the full orchestrator test suite. The cutover is behaviorally neutral because Step 2 preserved phase signaling (with strictly stronger failure semantics on the first transition).

## Phase 2: Worker Repo Becomes a Real uv Project

### Step 5: Pin the Python interpreter and dependency graph (`reigh-worker/pyproject.toml`)
**Scope:** Large
1. **Expand** `pyproject.toml` into a full PEP 621 project. Set `requires-python = ">=3.10,<3.11"` — single interpreter across all environments.
2. **Translate** dependencies from `requirements.txt` and `Wan2GP/requirements.txt` into `pyproject.toml`. Preserve every platform-specific wheel URL (e.g. `onnxruntime-gpu`, `insightface`) via `[tool.uv.sources]` / environment markers.
3. **Model** the torch split as extras: `cuda124` (Linux + RunPod) and `cuda128` (Windows optional).
4. **Preserve** existing pytest config and entrypoints verbatim.

### Step 6: Generate and validate `uv.lock` (`reigh-worker/uv.lock`, `reigh-worker/requirements.txt`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Generate** `uv.lock` via `uv lock --python 3.10`.
2. **Validate** resolution: `uv sync --locked --python 3.10 --extra cuda124` in a throwaway venv. Diff the installed package set against the current pip-based install; any drift must be pinned in `pyproject.toml` rather than silently upgrading.
3. **Keep** `requirements.txt` committed during rollout as rollback ballast with a comment marking it legacy.

## Phase 3: Command Surfaces Switch To `uv`

### Step 7: Rewrite client command builders (`reigh-app/src/shared/components/SettingsModal/commandUtils.ts`, `commandUtils.test.ts`)
**Scope:** Large
1. **Grep audit (first action):** re-run `rg "getInstallationCommand|getRunCommand"` across `reigh-app/src/`. Confirm only `GenerationTokenPanel.tsx` + the two test files call them. If a new caller has appeared, stop and switch to an `unsupported` sentinel return instead of a throw.
2. **Worker-repo path source (investigated and decided):** Verified via `rg` that `reigh-app/src/shared/components/SettingsModal/` currently has NO state field storing the worker-repo path (searched for `repoPath|workerPath|workerRepo|workerLocation|installPath|clonePath|repo_path|workerDirectory|worker_dir|Reigh-Worker|reigh-worker` and got no matches under `reigh-app/src/`). Therefore **option (a) is infeasible and option (b) is adopted**: a new `workerRepoPath` field is added to the Settings modal (see Step 8, now Medium scope) and threaded into `commandUtils.ts` via `CommandConfig`. The field's default values are `%USERPROFILE%\Reigh-Worker` on Windows and `/Users/user_c042661f/reigh-worker` on Linux. The absolute path the user configured is baked into every generated install/run command. Option (c) (hard-coded path) is explicitly rejected because it is hostile to users who already cloned elsewhere.
3. **Every generated install/run command MUST begin with a directory change into the configured worker repo**, before any uv installer or `uv sync`/`uv run` invocation — including first-time installs. The cd must come **before** the Astral installer step as well, because although the installer itself is cwd-agnostic, the subsequent `uv sync` must execute inside the repo so that `pyproject.toml`/`uv.lock` are discoverable. Concrete shapes:
   - **Linux (install and run):** prepend `cd "<WORKER_REPO_PATH>" && ` to the entire command chain.
   - **Windows PowerShell (install and run):** prepend `Set-Location -LiteralPath "<WORKER_REPO_PATH>"; ` to the entire command chain.
   - **Windows cmd (install and run):** prepend `cd /d "<WORKER_REPO_PATH>" && ` to the entire command chain. **The `/d` flag is MANDATORY** so the cd works across drive letters (e.g. user home on `C:` but worker repo on `D:`).
4. **Update** `commandUtils.ts`:
   - Extend `CommandConfig` to carry `workerRepoPath: string` (passed in from Settings state; see Step 8).
   - **Linux install:** `cd "<workerRepoPath>" && ` → `apt-get install python3.10-venv python3.10-dev ffmpeg` → `curl -LsSf https://astral.sh/uv/install.sh | sh` → `source $HOME/.local/bin/env` → one-time `venv/`/`.venv/` backup → `uv sync --locked --python 3.10 --extra cuda124` → `uv run --python 3.10 python run_worker.py ...`.
   - **Linux run:** `cd "<workerRepoPath>" && ` → `git pull` → `uv sync --locked --python 3.10 --extra cuda124` → `uv run --python 3.10 python run_worker.py ...`.
   - **Windows PowerShell install:** `Set-Location -LiteralPath "<workerRepoPath>"; ` → `powershell -ExecutionPolicy ByPass -c "irm https://astral.sh/uv/install.ps1 | iex"` → refresh PATH → `uv sync --locked --python 3.10 --extra cuda124|cuda128` → `uv run --python 3.10 python run_worker.py ...`.
   - **Windows PowerShell run:** `Set-Location -LiteralPath "<workerRepoPath>"; ` → `git pull` → `uv sync --locked --python 3.10 --extra cuda124|cuda128` → `uv run --python 3.10 python run_worker.py ...`.
   - **Windows cmd install:** `cd /d "<workerRepoPath>" && ` → run the PowerShell installer line from cmd, then **invoke uv via its explicit path** — `"%USERPROFILE%\.local\bin\uv.exe" sync --locked --python 3.10 --extra cuda124|cuda128` and `"%USERPROFILE%\.local\bin\uv.exe" run --python 3.10 python run_worker.py ...`. The `cd /d` sidesteps cwd-drift, and the explicit uv path sidesteps PATH propagation inside the running cmd session — both failure modes addressed.
   - **Windows cmd run:** `cd /d "<workerRepoPath>" && ` → `git pull` → `"%USERPROFILE%\.local\bin\uv.exe" sync --locked --python 3.10 --extra cuda124|cuda128` → `"%USERPROFILE%\.local\bin\uv.exe" run --python 3.10 python run_worker.py ...`.
5. **Change the macOS contract:** throw `UnsupportedPlatformError` for `computerType === "mac"`. Safe per sub-step 1 audit.
6. **Update** `commandUtils.test.ts`: Linux fresh install, Linux run, Windows PowerShell fresh install, **Windows cmd fresh install** (asserts the explicit `%USERPROFILE%\.local\bin\uv.exe` invocation), Windows PowerShell run, Windows cmd run, mac → throws. **Every non-mac test case MUST assert that the generated command starts with the appropriate cd-equivalent prefix AND contains the configured `workerRepoPath`.** Specifically:
   - Linux install/run cases assert the command begins with `cd "<workerRepoPath>" && `.
   - Windows PowerShell install/run cases assert the command begins with `Set-Location -LiteralPath "<workerRepoPath>"; `.
   - **Windows cmd install/run cases MUST specifically assert the literal string `cd /d` (with the `/d` flag present)** so any future regression where someone drops the `/d` is caught at test time.
   - Add a fixture `workerRepoPath` value (e.g. `C:\\Users\\MC\\Reigh-Worker` for Windows cases, `/home/tester/reigh-worker` for Linux cases) and assert it appears verbatim in the generated command.

### Step 8: Update Settings modal UX copy and add worker-repo path input (`GenerationTokenPanel.tsx`, `GenerationTokenPanel.test.tsx`, `SettingsModal.tsx`, `types.ts`, Settings persistence store)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Add a `Worker repo location` text input** to the Settings modal (co-located with the existing install/run command display, in `GenerationTokenPanel.tsx` or the adjacent section in `SettingsModal.tsx`). Bind it to a new `workerRepoPath` field on the Settings state type (`types.ts`) and persist it through the same store the rest of Settings uses. Defaults:
   - Windows: `%USERPROFILE%\Reigh-Worker` (resolved to an absolute path at render time if the store prefers absolute paths; otherwise leave the `%USERPROFILE%` token for cmd to expand and store a literal PowerShell-friendly variant separately only if testing shows cross-shell mismatch).
   - Linux: `/Users/user_c042661f/reigh-worker` (documented as "set this to wherever you cloned reigh-worker").
   - Include helper text: "The absolute path to your local clone of the reigh-worker repo. Every generated command will `cd` into this directory first, so commands work even if you open a fresh terminal in your home directory."
2. **Thread `workerRepoPath`** from Settings state into `CommandConfig` at the call site where `getInstallationCommand`/`getRunCommand` are invoked (`GenerationTokenPanel.tsx:147` area). Confirm via TypeScript that the new required field is propagated at every call site.
3. **Update** `GenerationTokenPanel.tsx` copy so users know every `Run` resyncs automatically and `Install` is first-time bootstrap + repair. Keep the two-tab model.
4. **Add a short Linux distro note** near the install command: "Requires Ubuntu 22.04 LTS / Debian 12 or a distro that packages `python3.10-venv`. On Ubuntu 24.04+ install the deadsnakes PPA first." (Doc-only; no generated-command change.)
5. **Update** `GenerationTokenPanel.test.tsx` for the new copy, for the new `workerRepoPath` input (renders, accepts input, persists), and assert mac still shows the unsupported message without calling the helpers. Add an explicit test that changing `workerRepoPath` in the Settings state causes the displayed install/run command to update with the new path in its `cd` prefix.

### Step 9: Rewrite orchestrator startup template (`gpu_orchestrator/runpod/worker_startup.template.sh`, `gpu_orchestrator/runpod/startup_script.py`, `gpu_orchestrator/runpod/lifecycle.py`)
**Scope:** Large
1. **Replace** the pip/venv bootstrap in `worker_startup.template.sh`:
   - Keep `apt-get install python3.10-venv python3.10-dev` as today.
   - `curl -LsSf https://astral.sh/uv/install.sh | sh` if `uv` missing; `source $HOME/.local/bin/env`.
   - One-time `venv/` backup + sentinel.
   - `git pull`.
   - **`uv sync --locked --python 3.10 --extra cuda124`** — the `--python 3.10` flag is mandatory so uv cannot pick up the base image's `/usr/bin/python3.11`.
   - Launch via `uv run --python 3.10 python worker.py --supabase-url ... --worker $WORKER_ID ...` with CLI args byte-for-byte identical to current line 328.
2. **Wire** the phase-write helper from Phase 1 Step 2 around the new steps: `deps_installing` (retry-then-exit strict) before `uv sync`, `deps_verified` (lenient) after, `worker_starting` (lenient) before launch, `ready` (lenient) at the same point as today.
3. **Remove** the hash-based pip repair block — unconditional `uv sync --locked` replaces it.
4. **Stop on `uv sync` failure** with phase left at `deps_installing` (which by construction will have succeeded in Step 2, because otherwise the pod would have already exited). The `uv sync` failure itself exits non-zero and the worker never starts.
5. **Touch** `startup_script.py` and `lifecycle.py` only if rendered-template inputs or log parsing patterns need updating.

### Step 10: Orchestrator startup tests (`tests/gpu_orchestrator/runpod/test_startup_script.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Assert** the rendered template contains: `apt-get install python3.10-venv`, uv installer invocation, venv backup block, `uv sync --locked --python 3.10 --extra cuda124`, launch line `uv run --python 3.10 python worker.py ...` with unchanged CLI args, and the strict `deps_installing` retry-then-exit helper.

## Phase 4: Migration, Docs, Rollback

### Step 11: One-time migration sentinel (implemented inside Steps 7 and 9)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Write** a repo-local sentinel (e.g. `.uv-migrated`) only after the first successful `uv sync`.
2. **Behavior:** on launch, if the sentinel is absent, rename any existing `venv/` → `venv.pre-uv-<timestamp>` and `.venv/` → `.venv.pre-uv-<timestamp>` before running `uv sync`. After sync succeeds, write the sentinel. If sync fails, the sentinel is not written, backups remain on disk, and re-running retries the migration.
3. **Leave** backups on disk until the user removes them; no auto-cleanup.

### Step 12: Update owned docs and debug helpers (`reigh-worker/README.md`, `reigh-worker/STRUCTURE.md`, `reigh-worker/debug/README.md`, `reigh-worker/docs/KIJAI_SVI_IMPLEMENTATION.md`, `reigh-worker-orchestrator/scripts/debug/README.md`, `reigh-app/scripts/debug/commands/pod.py`, `docs/structure_detail/task_worker_lifecycle.md`, `reigh-app/docs/structure_detail/task_worker_lifecycle.md`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Rewrite** the worker quick-start in `README.md` for the uv flow; pin `--python 3.10` in every example; name the supported Linux baseline (Ubuntu 22.04 / Debian 12) and point Ubuntu 24.04+ users at deadsnakes as a manual prerequisite.
2. **Update** `STRUCTURE.md` with (a) the uv flow and (b) a short "Vendored Wan2GP subtree drift" note explaining that Wan2GP dependencies now live in Reigh's `pyproject.toml` and future subtree pulls require manual re-translation.
3. **Update** `debug/README.md`, `docs/KIJAI_SVI_IMPLEMENTATION.md`, `scripts/debug/README.md`, and `pod.py` to remove stale pip/venv instructions.
4. **Update BOTH** copies of `task_worker_lifecycle.md`. Consolidation of the duplication is out of scope.
5. **Do not touch** vendored Wan2GP upstream docs.

### Step 13: Rollback plan
**Scope:** Small
1. **Runtime rollback during first migration:** rename `venv.pre-uv-<timestamp>` back into place if the first `uv sync` fails destructively.
2. **No pip fallback at runtime.** If `uv sync` fails post-migration, fail loudly with logs.
3. **Release rollback:** reverting commits that touch `commandUtils.ts`, `worker_startup.template.sh`, `pyproject.toml`, `uv.lock`, and the import cutover restores the pip/venv flow because `requirements.txt` is still committed.

## Execution Order
1. **Phase 1 first** — port `startup_phase` writes into the template-backed path with the retry-then-exit strict helper, then cut imports over. No uv changes yet; no orchestrator control-loop changes; this must be behaviorally neutral aside from failing visibly instead of silently on the first PATCH.
2. **Phase 2** — land `pyproject.toml` + `uv.lock` in the worker repo so downstream commands have a real target.
3. **Phase 3** — update client command builders (after grep audit) and orchestrator startup template in the same release window.
4. **Phase 4** — migration sentinel (already inside Steps 7/9), then docs, then rollback drill.

## Validation Order
1. **Cheapest first:** `uv lock --python 3.10` resolution; `commandUtils.test.ts` including new cmd install cases asserting the explicit `%USERPROFILE%\.local\bin\uv.exe` invocation; `test_startup_script.py` including new phase-sequence and strict-retry assertions.
2. **Fresh-install smoke tests:**
   - Linux local (Ubuntu 22.04, Python 3.10) cuda124.
   - Windows PowerShell (Python 3.10) cuda124.
   - Windows PowerShell (Python 3.10) cuda128.
   - **Windows cmd (Python 3.10) cuda124** — verify the very next command after the installer uses `%USERPROFILE%\.local\bin\uv.exe` directly.
   - RunPod (Python 3.10) cuda124 — assert the resulting `.venv/pyvenv.cfg` interpreter is 3.10 (not the base image 3.11), i.e. uv honored `--python 3.10`.
   - **Wrong-cwd acceptance smoke test (MANDATORY, Phase 3 — not deferred to Phase 4):** Open a fresh terminal in a directory OTHER than the worker repo (specifically the user's home directory, e.g. `C:\Users\<name>` on Windows cmd, `C:\Users\<name>` on Windows PowerShell, `$HOME` on Linux). Copy-paste the generated install command from the Settings modal. Verify it succeeds end-to-end. If it fails because the cwd was wrong (e.g. `No pyproject.toml found in current directory or any parent directory`), the migration has not solved the original transcript bug and is a no-go. Run the same test for the generated run command. Repeat for Windows cmd specifically because `cd` vs `cd /d` is a drive-letter-boundary failure mode.
3. **Upgrade smoke tests:**
   - Linux local upgrade from existing `venv/`.
   - Linux local upgrade from existing `.venv/`.
   - Windows PowerShell upgrade from existing `venv/`.
   - Windows cmd upgrade from existing `venv/`.
   - **RunPod upgrade from existing `venv/` on mounted workspace** — existing venv built under 3.10, new env must also resolve to 3.10; explicitly assert `onnxruntime-gpu` and `insightface` still import.
4. **macOS guardrail:** `GenerationTokenPanel` still shows the unsupported message and never calls the helpers; direct mac invocation of `getInstallationCommand`/`getRunCommand` throws.
5. **Failure paths:**
   - `uv` missing but installer reachable.
   - Network down during `uv sync` after successful prior migration → the `uv sync` itself exits non-zero; pod is visibly failed.
   - **First `deps_installing` PATCH unreachable** → pod retries 3× then `exit 1` before running `uv sync`; RunPod console shows the failed pod; `worker_state.py`'s existing 30-minute startup safety net at line 200-204 eventually reaps it. No orchestrator-side change required.
   - Corrupted `uv.lock` stops startup with actionable logs.

## Watch Items
- **PATH propagation and cwd are two separate failure modes — both must be eliminated.** The Windows cmd explicit-`uv.exe`-path fix (in Step 7 / settled decision `cmd-explicit-uv-path`) only eliminates PATH propagation; it does nothing about cwd. The cd-prefix amendment (Step 7 sub-step 3) eliminates cwd drift. **The whole migration's success criterion is that a non-technical Windows user who opens `cmd.exe` in `C:\Users\<name>` (their home directory, NOT the repo) can copy-paste the generated command and have it work end-to-end.** If either failure mode resurfaces — a future refactor drops the cd prefix, or someone removes the `/d` flag on Windows cmd, or PATH is assumed to contain `uv` after the installer — the migration has regressed to the original transcript bug. The wrong-cwd smoke test in Validation Order is the gating acceptance test for this criterion.

        Existing flags:
        [
  {
    "id": "FLAG-012",
    "concern": "Strict helper must verify HTTP response, not just curl exit: Phase 1 Step 2's `update_worker_phase_strict` variant is described as 'curl ... || return 1' instead of '|| return 0'. But curl exits 0 on HTTP 4xx/5xx by default, so a Supabase 500 would still be reported as success and the retry loop would never fire. The plan should specify `curl --fail` (or explicit status-code checking via -w '%{http_code}') in the strict variant so the retry actually triggers on server errors, not just on network failures.",
    "status": "open",
    "severity": "minor"
  },
  {
    "id": "FLAG-013",
    "concern": "30-minute startup cap may be tight for a cold uv sync: If deps_verified PATCH silently fails after deps_installing succeeds, startup_phase stays at 'deps_installing' and worker_state.py:200-204's 30-minute cap applies. A cold `uv sync --extra cuda124` that has to download torch wheels plus the full Wan2GP dep tree on a fresh pod can approach or exceed this window. The plan retains lenient semantics for subsequent phase writes without a benchmark confirming the 30-minute cap is comfortably above typical sync time, so a transient network blip during deps_verified could cause a healthy pod to be reaped as STARTUP_PHASE_TIMEOUT.",
    "status": "open",
    "severity": "minor"
  },
  {
    "id": "FLAG-014",
    "concern": "Apt package unavailability produces confusing error: Ubuntu 24.04+ users who skip the deadsnakes PPA documentation will get `E: Unable to locate package python3.10-venv` from apt mid-installation. The generated install command could add a one-line precheck (`apt-cache show python3.10-venv >/dev/null 2>&1 || { echo 'python3.10 not available \u2014 install deadsnakes PPA first'; exit 1; }`) that converts the failure into an actionable message. The plan relies entirely on documentation, which users may skip.",
    "status": "open",
    "severity": "minor"
  }
]

      "id": "DEBT-105",
      "concern": "dependency resolution: the plan does not lock down the active python-version matrix even though the repo currently splits between python 3.10 local installs and a python 3.11 runpod image.",

      ]
    }
  ],
  "did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-013",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the package is internally inconsistent. the plan body says to keep `activepairdata` in `usestate` and make phase 2 optional, but the attached metadata and success criteria still describe the previous derive-via-`usememo` / remove-`setactivepairdata` / simplify-`onpairclick` plan. that means the approved-plan requirements are only partially coherent as presented.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-108",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the linux distro note is documentation-only; the generated install command in step 7 still runs `apt-get install python3.10-venv python3.10-dev ffmpeg` without any pre-check that the package exists. users on ubuntu 24.04+ who ignore the doc and try to copy-paste the command will hit a confusing `e: unable to locate package python3.10-venv` from apt rather than a targeted error from commandutils.ts. a one-line detection pre-check (e.g., `apt-cache show python3.10-venv >/dev/null 2>&1 || { echo 'install deadsnakes ppa first \u2014 see readme'; exit 1; }`) would convert the silent failure into an actionable error, but the plan does not add this.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-014",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: phase 1 still misses a real supporting-data location: `trailingpairdata` lives outside `pairdatabyindex` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotpresentationadapter.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotpresentationadapter.ts#l111), but the proposed sync logic only considers `pairdatabyindex`. if trailing slot regenerate is part of the bug surface, that location is not covered.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-110",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the non-swallowing helper `update_worker_phase_strict` is a new shell function, but phase 1 step 2 introduces it only as pseudo-code. there is no concrete note that the helper must handle supabase http non-2xx responses as failures (curl returns 0 even on http 4xx/5xx by default unless `-f` or `--fail-with-body` is used). if the implementer ports `update_worker_phase` verbatim with only `|| return 0` removed, a 500 from supabase would still report success because curl's exit code is 0. the plan should specify `curl --fail` or explicit http status code checking in the strict variant.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-109",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the step 2 retry loop uses a naked `sleep 2` between attempts, which adds up to 6 seconds of latency on the happy path when the first patch eventually succeeds on attempt 2 or 3. for pods where supabase is reachable but slow during the initial moments of a cold runpod start, the retry logic is fine. however, if the first attempt fails with a tls handshake delay and the retry loop sleeps 2s per attempt regardless of whether curl itself has been blocking for tens of seconds, the total startup latency penalty could be substantial. this is a minor tuning concern, not a correctness issue.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "hook-abstraction": [
    {

  "is-the-change-in-the-right-place-and-would-it-break-any-callers": [

      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "is-the-scope-and-scale-of-the-change-appropriate": [

  {
    "subsystem": "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct",
    "total_occurrences": 5,
    "plan_count": 2,
    "entries": [
      {
        "id": "DEBT-007",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: for ordinary pairs, the new diagnosis is not well-supported by the repo. the current sync effect in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l62) already syncs `url` and `primaryvariantid`, and the regenerate path reads exactly `starturl`, `endurl`, `startgenid`, `endgenid`, `startvariantid`, and `endvariantid` from `segmentslotmode.pairdata` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts#l438). adding `thumburl` and `generationid` to the sync does not, by itself, explain why an old image url is still being used for regular pairs.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-008",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the fix is incomplete for extra trailing slots. `handlepairclick` can populate `activepairdata` from `trailingpairdata` when `pairindex === pairdatabyindex.size` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l121), but the proposed sync effect only reads `pairdatabyindex.get(segmentslotlightboxindex)`. for those trailing-slot cases `fresh` is `undefined`, so no image refresh happens.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-018",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the merge pseudocode does not actually sync 'all image fields' as claimed. it omits `id` and `position` from change detection, and when `fresh.startimage` or `fresh.endimage` becomes `null` it preserves the stale previous object instead of syncing that null state. as written, it also needs explicit null-guards around spreads of `prev.startimage` / `prev.endimage` to be safe in strict typescript.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-106",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan's claim that `worker_state.py:200-204` (the 30-minute startup safety net) catches abandoned pods that exited before patching is slightly imprecise. line 200-204 only runs when `in_startup_phase` is true, which requires `startup_phase` to be in `('deps_installing', 'deps_verified', 'worker_starting')` per line 161. a pod that exits before its first patch has `startup_phase = none`, so `in_startup_phase` is false and the code falls through to the normal timeout checks (active_stale / not_claiming) at line 207+, not the 30-minute startup cap. both paths eventually reap the worker, so the end result is the same, but the overview's reasoning (naming 200-204 specifically) is wrong. worth correcting so future readers don't mis-reason about the invariant.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-107",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked worker_state.py line 161 (`in_startup_phase = startup_phase in (...)`) against the plan's assumption that subsequent lenient writes are safe. if deps_installing succeeds but deps_verified silently fails, startup_phase stays at 'deps_installing' and the 30-minute cap from line 200-204 applies \u2014 even though the pod is actually progressing through `uv sync`. on a slow runpod image with cold wheel downloads, `uv sync --extra cuda124` plus wan2gp deps can take 20+ minutes. there's no concrete benchmark in the plan that says the 30-minute cap is safely above the expected sync time; if `uv sync` legitimately takes 25 minutes and the deps_verified patch fails on a transient network blip, the orchestrator will terminate a healthy pod. plan should either bump the cap or note that deps_verified swallowing is acceptable because the cap is generously above typical sync time.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
        ]
      }
    ]
  },

        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: Migrate Worker Bootstrap From pip/venv to uv

## Overview
Migrate the Python worker runtime from pip/venv to `uv` with a committed lockfile, and run `uv sync --locked` on every launch across the client Settings modal, the orchestrator RunPod startup template, and the worker repo itself.

Current shape verified in the repo:
- [reigh-worker/pyproject.toml](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker/pyproject.toml) exists but is not the runtime source of truth; installs come from [requirements.txt](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker/requirements.txt) and the vendored [Wan2GP/requirements.txt](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker/Wan2GP/requirements.txt).
- Client install commands in [commandUtils.ts:53,66](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/shared/components/SettingsModal/commandUtils.ts) currently begin with `git clone --depth 1 https://github.com/banodoco/Reigh-Worker.git` then `cd Reigh-Worker` — this is the first-time bootstrap contract and **must be preserved** after migration.
- The persisted launch-config seam is [useWorkerLaunchConfig.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/shared/components/SettingsModal/hooks/useWorkerLaunchConfig.ts), which today owns 6 fields (`computerType`, `gpuType`, `memoryProfile`, `windowsShell`, `showDebugLogs`, `idleReleaseMinutes`) via `usePersistentState`. `WorkerLaunchConfigValues` and `WorkerLaunchConfigSetters` are the interfaces that must grow a new `workerRepoPath` field. **`SettingsModal.tsx` is NOT the persistence seam** — it only consumes the hook. [sections/GenerationSection/GenerationSection.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/shared/components/SettingsModal/sections/GenerationSection/GenerationSection.tsx) forwards `launchConfig`/`launchSetters` into `GenerationTokenPanel`, which constructs the `CommandConfig` passed to `commandUtils`.
- Both the live [runpod_client.py:1336](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker-orchestrator/gpu_orchestrator/runpod_client.py) and the template-backed [worker_startup.template.sh:328](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker-orchestrator/gpu_orchestrator/runpod/worker_startup.template.sh) launch `python worker.py` directly.
- **RunPod actually runs Python 3.10.** Base image is `runpod/pytorch:2.4.0-py3.11-...` ([runpod_client.py:357](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker-orchestrator/gpu_orchestrator/runpod_client.py)), but both `runpod_client.py:985/993` and `worker_startup.template.sh:105/113` apt-install `python3.10-venv` and explicitly create the runtime venv with `python3.10 -m venv`.
- [runpod_client.py:890-922](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker-orchestrator/gpu_orchestrator/runpod_client.py) defines `update_worker_phase`, which writes `metadata.startup_phase` via Supabase REST but silently swallows all errors.
- [worker_state.py:140](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker-orchestrator/gpu_orchestrator/worker_state.py) reads `startup_phase` from an in-memory dict populated by Supabase polling. There is NO pod-file reader anywhere in the orchestrator control loop.
- `getInstallationCommand`/`getRunCommand` are referenced in exactly four files: `commandUtils.ts`, `commandUtils.test.ts`, `GenerationTokenPanel.tsx`, `GenerationTokenPanel.test.tsx`. `GenerationTokenPanel` is the only non-test caller and already guards `computerType === "mac"`.
- Legacy `runpod_client` references live in [scripts/debug/commands/storage.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker-orchestrator/scripts/debug/commands/storage.py), [gpu_orchestrator/logging_config.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker-orchestrator/gpu_orchestrator/logging_config.py), [api_orchestrator/logging_config.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker-orchestrator/api_orchestrator/logging_config.py).
- `task_worker_lifecycle.md` is duplicated under both [docs/structure_detail/](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/docs/structure_detail/task_worker_lifecycle.md) and [reigh-app/docs/structure_detail/](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/docs/structure_detail/task_worker_lifecycle.md).

### Scope guardrails (pinned decisions)
- **Python matrix:** Linux local = 3.10, Windows local = 3.10, RunPod = 3.10. `pyproject.toml` declares `requires-python = ">=3.10,<3.11"`. Every `uv sync`, `uv run`, and `uv lock` pins `--python 3.10` explicitly.
- **Linux distro scope + precheck:** Supported baseline is Ubuntu 22.04 LTS / Debian 12 (distros that ship `python3.10-venv` natively). The generated Linux install command includes an **apt-cache precheck** (`apt-cache show python3.10-venv >/dev/null 2>&1 || { echo 'ERROR: python3.10-venv not in apt; install deadsnakes PPA first — see README'; exit 1; }`) so Ubuntu 24.04+ users hit an actionable error instead of a generic apt failure. Docs (`README.md`, `GenerationTokenPanel` help copy) point them at deadsnakes.
- **Process model:** orchestrator template keeps `python worker.py` as the launch line (wrapped as `uv run --python 3.10 python worker.py ...`).
- **Canonical bootstrap:** Reigh-owned `reigh-worker/pyproject.toml` + `uv.lock` is the single source of truth. Vendored `Wan2GP/scripts/install.*` and `setup.py` out of scope. **Accepted debt:** Wan2GP subtree drift documented in `reigh-worker/STRUCTURE.md`.
- **macOS contract:** throw `UnsupportedPlatformError` for mac. Verified safe because `GenerationTokenPanel` is the only non-test caller and already guards. **Accepted debt:** future `NonMacComputerType` narrow type would prevent regression; deferred.
- **Worker-repo path (user amendment):** A new persisted `workerRepoPath` field is added to `WorkerLaunchConfigValues` / `WorkerLaunchConfigSetters` in `useWorkerLaunchConfig.ts`. Defaults: `%USERPROFILE%\Reigh-Worker` (Windows), `$HOME/reigh-worker` (Linux). Threaded through `GenerationSection.tsx` → `GenerationTokenPanel.tsx` → `CommandConfig`.
- **Install commands begin with `git clone` then cd (user amendment + FLAG-015 fix):** Every install command begins with a clone into `<workerRepoPath>` (**idempotent**, so re-running against an existing clone is a no-op), then cd into that path, then the rest. Run commands do NOT clone — they cd into the existing path, `git pull`, then sync. This eliminates BOTH failure modes from the original transcript bug (wrong-cwd AND missing clone).
- **Phase signaling:** Phase 1 hardens only the template side. First `deps_installing` PATCH uses a retry-then-exit loop with concrete `curl --fail` flags. Subsequent phase writes stay lenient. NO orchestrator control-loop changes.
- **Startup timeout safety (FLAG-013 closure):** Phase 2 Step 6 includes a benchmark sub-step measuring cold-cache `uv sync --python 3.10 --extra cuda124` wall time on RunPod. If p95 > 20 min (within 30% of the existing 30-min `worker_state.py:200` cap), the plan bumps `max_startup_phase_sec` to 2700 (45 min) in Step 9. Deferred cap-bump is tracked by a concrete sub-step, not as floating debt.

## Phase 1: Preserve Startup-Phase Invariant In the Template Path

### Step 1: Document the startup_phase contract (`gpu_orchestrator/runpod_client.py`, `gpu_orchestrator/worker_state.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Enumerate** every `metadata.startup_phase` value the live orchestrator writes and reads across `runpod_client.py:890-922` and `worker_state.py:140,161,201`.
2. **Audit** the four `|| return 0` / `|| true` swallow points in `update_worker_phase`.

### Step 2: Port startup_phase writes into the template with concrete `curl --fail` retry-then-exit on first PATCH (`gpu_orchestrator/runpod/worker_startup.template.sh`, `gpu_orchestrator/runpod/startup_script.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Port** the `update_worker_phase` shell helper into `worker_startup.template.sh` at identical transition points. Keep pip/venv install steps intact in this step.
2. **Define `update_worker_phase_strict`** with concrete curl flags (addresses FLAG-012/correctness-1):
   ```sh
   update_worker_phase_strict() {
       local phase="$1"
       local key="$SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY"
       [ -z "$key" ] && return 1  # strict: missing key is a hard failure, not silent
       local http_code
       http_code=$(curl --silent --show-error --output /dev/null \
           --write-out '%{http_code}' \
           --max-time 10 \
           --retry 0 \
           -X PATCH \
           "${SUPABASE_URL}/rest/v1/workers?id=eq.${WORKER_ID}" \
           -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
           -H "Authorization: Bearer $key" \
           -H "apikey: $key" \
           -H "Prefer: return=minimal" \
           -d "{\"metadata\":{\"startup_phase\":\"$phase\"}}") || return 1
       # curl exits 0 on HTTP 4xx/5xx by default; explicit code check required.
       case "$http_code" in
           2*) return 0 ;;
           *) echo "update_worker_phase_strict: HTTP $http_code" >&2 ; return 1 ;;
       esac
   }
   ```
   Note the explicit `%{http_code}` parsing — `curl --fail` alone is insufficient because a transient 5xx body with no retry budget would still exit non-zero in a way that conflates network errors with HTTP errors; explicit code check is cleaner and testable.
3. **Wrap the first `deps_installing` PATCH in bounded retry + hard exit**:
   ```sh
   for attempt in 1 2 3; do
       if update_worker_phase_strict "deps_installing"; then
           break
       fi
       if [ "$attempt" = "3" ]; then
           echo "❌ FATAL: deps_installing PATCH failed 3× (network, auth, or Supabase 5xx); aborting so RunPod console surfaces the failure" >&2
           exit 1
       fi
       sleep 2
   done
   ```
4. **Subsequent phase writes (`deps_verified`, `worker_starting`, `ready`)** retain the legacy lenient helper — the pod has already proven it can reach Supabase by then.
5. **Rationale:** If the first PATCH fails permanently, the pod `exit 1`s before `uv sync`. `worker_state.py:200-204`'s existing 30-minute safety net reaps the abandoned worker. **No orchestrator-side change required.**
6. **Parameterize** the phase-update target in `startup_script.py` if the rendered template needs an endpoint/token.

### Step 3: Template-side parity tests only (`tests/gpu_orchestrator/runpod/test_startup_script.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Extend** `test_startup_script.py` to assert the rendered template: (a) writes each phase at the correct sequence points; (b) the strict helper uses `--write-out '%{http_code}'` and explicit `2*)` case matching; (c) the first `deps_installing` branch has the 3-attempt retry loop and `exit 1` path; (d) subsequent phase writes remain lenient.
2. **Do NOT modify** `tests/gpu_orchestrator/control/phases/test_state.py` or `test_periodic.py`.

### Step 4: Cut active callers over to `gpu_orchestrator.runpod` (`gpu_orchestrator/control_loop.py`, `scripts/spawn_gpu.py`, `scripts/shutdown_all_workers.py`, `scripts/ssh_to_worker.py`, `scripts/terminate_single_worker.py`, `scripts/debug/services/workers.py`, `scripts/debug/commands/storage.py`, `gpu_orchestrator/logging_config.py`, `api_orchestrator/logging_config.py`, `tests/test_runpod_decomposition.py`, `tests/scripts/test_debug_services.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Switch** imports from `gpu_orchestrator.runpod_client` to `gpu_orchestrator.runpod`.
2. **Update** both logging allowlists.
3. **Leave** `runpod_client.py` as an unreferenced compatibility shim.
4. **Run** the full orchestrator test suite.

## Phase 2: Worker Repo Becomes a Real uv Project

### Step 5: Pin the Python interpreter and dependency graph (`reigh-worker/pyproject.toml`)
**Scope:** Large
1. **Expand** `pyproject.toml` into a full PEP 621 project. Set `requires-python = ">=3.10,<3.11"`.
2. **Translate** dependencies from `requirements.txt` and `Wan2GP/requirements.txt` into `pyproject.toml`. Preserve every platform-specific wheel URL via `[tool.uv.sources]` / environment markers.
3. **Model** the torch split as extras: `cuda124` (Linux + RunPod) and `cuda128` (Windows optional).
4. **Preserve** existing pytest config and entrypoints verbatim.

### Step 6: Generate, validate, and benchmark `uv.lock` (`reigh-worker/uv.lock`, `reigh-worker/requirements.txt`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Generate** `uv.lock` via `uv lock --python 3.10`.
2. **Validate** resolution: `uv sync --locked --python 3.10 --extra cuda124` in a throwaway venv. Diff against the current pip-based install; pin drift in `pyproject.toml`.
3. **Benchmark cold-cache wall time (closes FLAG-013):** on a fresh RunPod pod with no torch cache, time `uv sync --locked --python 3.10 --extra cuda124`. Record p50 and an approximate p95 across 3 runs. If p95 > 20 minutes, **Step 9 MUST bump `max_startup_phase_sec` in `gpu_orchestrator/worker_state.py` from 1800 to 2700 (45 min)** and the benchmark result is recorded in the PR description. If p95 ≤ 20 minutes, the existing 30-minute cap stands and no `worker_state.py` change is needed. Either way, the benchmark closes the debt.
4. **Keep** `requirements.txt` committed during rollout as rollback ballast.

## Phase 3: Command Surfaces Switch To `uv`

### Step 7: Rewrite client command builders with `git clone` + cd + uv (`reigh-app/src/shared/components/SettingsModal/commandUtils.ts`, `commandUtils.test.ts`)
**Scope:** Large
1. **Grep audit (first action):** re-run `rg "getInstallationCommand|getRunCommand"` across `reigh-app/src/`. Confirm only `GenerationTokenPanel.tsx` + the two test files call them.
2. **Extend `CommandConfig`** to carry `workerRepoPath: string` (supplied from the launch config; see Step 8).
3. **Install commands start with git clone, then cd, then the rest.** The clone is **idempotent** via a preguard so re-running against an existing clone is a no-op. Run commands start with cd + pull (no clone).
4. **Concrete shapes:**
   - **Linux install:**
     ```sh
     [ -d "<workerRepoPath>/.git" ] || git clone --depth 1 https://github.com/banodoco/Reigh-Worker.git "<workerRepoPath>" && \
     cd "<workerRepoPath>" && \
     apt-cache show python3.10-venv >/dev/null 2>&1 || { echo "ERROR: python3.10-venv not in apt; install deadsnakes PPA first — see README"; exit 1; } && \
     sudo apt-get update && sudo apt-get install -y python3.10-venv python3.10-dev ffmpeg && \
     curl -LsSf https://astral.sh/uv/install.sh | sh && \
     source $HOME/.local/bin/env && \
     { [ -d venv ] && mv venv "venv.pre-uv-$(date +%s)" || true; } && \
     { [ -d .venv ] && mv .venv ".venv.pre-uv-$(date +%s)" || true; } && \
     uv sync --locked --python 3.10 --extra cuda124 && \
     touch .uv-migrated && \
     uv run --python 3.10 python run_worker.py ...
     ```
   - **Linux run:**
     ```sh
     cd "<workerRepoPath>" && \
     git pull && \
     uv sync --locked --python 3.10 --extra cuda124 && \
     uv run --python 3.10 python run_worker.py ...
     ```
   - **Windows PowerShell install:**
     ```powershell
     if (-not (Test-Path "<workerRepoPath>\.git")) { git clone --depth 1 https://github.com/banodoco/Reigh-Worker.git "<workerRepoPath>" }
     Set-Location -LiteralPath "<workerRepoPath>"
     powershell -ExecutionPolicy ByPass -c "irm https://astral.sh/uv/install.ps1 | iex"
     $env:Path = [System.Environment]::GetEnvironmentVariable('Path','User') + ';' + $env:Path
     uv sync --locked --python 3.10 --extra cuda124
     uv run --python 3.10 python run_worker.py ...
     ```
   - **Windows PowerShell run:** `Set-Location -LiteralPath "<workerRepoPath>"; git pull; uv sync --locked --python 3.10 --extra cuda124; uv run --python 3.10 python run_worker.py ...`
   - **Windows cmd install:**
     ```bat
     if not exist "<workerRepoPath>\.git" git clone --depth 1 https://github.com/banodoco/Reigh-Worker.git "<workerRepoPath>"
     cd /d "<workerRepoPath>"
     powershell -ExecutionPolicy ByPass -c "irm https://astral.sh/uv/install.ps1 | iex"
     "%USERPROFILE%\.local\bin\uv.exe" sync --locked --python 3.10 --extra cuda124
     "%USERPROFILE%\.local\bin\uv.exe" run --python 3.10 python run_worker.py ...
     ```
     Note: `cd /d` is MANDATORY (cross-drive-letter); `%USERPROFILE%\.local\bin\uv.exe` is used explicitly so no PATH propagation is needed inside the running cmd session.
   - **Windows cmd run:** `cd /d "<workerRepoPath>" && git pull && "%USERPROFILE%\.local\bin\uv.exe" sync --locked --python 3.10 --extra cuda124 && "%USERPROFILE%\.local\bin\uv.exe" run --python 3.10 python run_worker.py ...`
5. **Drop `python3.10`** from bare prerequisites where uv manages the env; keep only where still needed (Linux apt-cache precheck above).
6. **Change the macOS contract:** throw `UnsupportedPlatformError` for `computerType === "mac"`.
7. **Update** `commandUtils.test.ts`. For every non-mac case:
   - Assert install commands begin with the correct idempotent-clone pattern (`[ -d "<workerRepoPath>/.git" ]` on Linux, `if (-not (Test-Path ...))` on PowerShell, `if not exist "<workerRepoPath>\.git"` on cmd) followed by a `git clone --depth 1 https://github.com/banodoco/Reigh-Worker.git "<workerRepoPath>"`.
   - Assert install commands then cd into `<workerRepoPath>` (Linux: `cd "<workerRepoPath>" && `; PowerShell: `Set-Location -LiteralPath "<workerRepoPath>"`; **cmd: literal `cd /d` — test explicitly asserts the `/d` flag is present** so a future regression that drops `/d` is caught at test time).
   - Assert run commands begin with the cd-equivalent (no clone).
   - Assert Linux install contains `apt-cache show python3.10-venv` precheck.
   - Assert Windows cmd uses `"%USERPROFILE%\.local\bin\uv.exe"` (explicit path).
   - Use fixture `workerRepoPath` values: `C:\\Users\\MC\\Reigh-Worker` (Windows), `/home/tester/reigh-worker` (Linux). Assert the path appears verbatim in both the clone argument and the cd argument.
   - mac case: asserts `throw UnsupportedPlatformError`.

### Step 8: Thread `workerRepoPath` through the persistence pipeline (`reigh-app/src/shared/components/SettingsModal/hooks/useWorkerLaunchConfig.ts`, `reigh-app/src/shared/components/SettingsModal/sections/GenerationSection/GenerationSection.tsx`, `reigh-app/src/shared/components/SettingsModal/sections/GenerationSection/components/GenerationTokenPanel.tsx`, `GenerationTokenPanel.test.tsx`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Add `workerRepoPath` to `WorkerLaunchConfigValues` and `WorkerLaunchConfigSetters`** in [useWorkerLaunchConfig.ts:7-23](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/shared/components/SettingsModal/hooks/useWorkerLaunchConfig.ts). Register a 7th `usePersistentState<string>("worker-repo-path", defaultWorkerRepoPath())` where `defaultWorkerRepoPath()` returns `"%USERPROFILE%\\Reigh-Worker"` on Windows or `"$HOME/reigh-worker"` on Linux (decided by the same `computerType` logic already in the hook or, if unavailable there, a simple navigator-based branch). Audit and fix any place that spreads `config` explicitly.
