Every generated command will cd into this directory first so commands work even if you open a fresh terminal in your home directory. Leave the default if you haven't cloned yet — the install command will clone it for you."
4. **Add a short Linux distro note** near the install command: "Requires Ubuntu 22.04 LTS / Debian 12 or a distro that packages `python3.10-venv`. On Ubuntu 24.04+ install the deadsnakes PPA first."
5. **Construct `CommandConfig`** at the `getInstallationCommand`/`getRunCommand` call site in `GenerationTokenPanel.tsx` with `workerRepoPath: launchConfig.workerRepoPath`. TypeScript will reject any other call site that misses it.
6. **Update** `GenerationTokenPanel.test.tsx` for the new copy, for the new input (renders, accepts input, persists), and for the mac unsupported message without calling the helpers. Add a test that changing `workerRepoPath` in Settings state causes the displayed install and run commands to update with the new path in their clone arg and cd arg.
7. **Keep** the two-tab model (Install = first-time clone + bootstrap + repair; Run = pull + resync + launch every time).

### Step 9: Rewrite orchestrator startup template (`gpu_orchestrator/runpod/worker_startup.template.sh`, `gpu_orchestrator/runpod/startup_script.py`, `gpu_orchestrator/runpod/lifecycle.py`, conditionally `gpu_orchestrator/worker_state.py`)
**Scope:** Large
1. **Replace** the pip/venv bootstrap in `worker_startup.template.sh`:
   - Keep `apt-get install python3.10-venv python3.10-dev`.
   - `curl -LsSf https://astral.sh/uv/install.sh | sh` if `uv` missing; `source $HOME/.local/bin/env`.
   - One-time `venv/` backup + `.uv-migrated` sentinel.
   - `git pull`.
   - **`uv sync --locked --python 3.10 --extra cuda124`**.
   - Launch via `uv run --python 3.10 python worker.py ...` with CLI args byte-for-byte identical to current line 328.
2. **Wire** the phase helper from Step 2: strict retry-then-exit before `uv sync`, lenient writes after.
3. **Remove** the hash-based pip repair block.
4. **Conditional `worker_state.py` change (gated on Step 6 benchmark):** if and only if Step 6's p95 cold-sync time exceeds 20 minutes, update `max_startup_phase_sec = 1800` at `worker_state.py:200` to `2700`. Otherwise leave unchanged. This is the ONLY orchestrator control-loop change permitted in this migration, and it is gated on measured data.
5. **Touch** `startup_script.py` and `lifecycle.py` only if rendered-template inputs or log parsing patterns need updating.

### Step 10: Orchestrator startup tests (`tests/gpu_orchestrator/runpod/test_startup_script.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Assert** the rendered template contains: `apt-get install python3.10-venv`, uv installer invocation, venv backup block, `uv sync --locked --python 3.10 --extra cuda124`, launch line `uv run --python 3.10 python worker.py ...`, and the strict `deps_installing` retry-then-exit helper with explicit HTTP code check.

## Phase 4: Migration, Docs, Rollback

### Step 11: One-time migration sentinel (implemented inside Steps 7 and 9)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Write** `.uv-migrated` only after the first successful `uv sync`.
2. **Behavior:** on launch, if the sentinel is absent, rename existing `venv/` → `venv.pre-uv-<timestamp>` and `.venv/` → `.venv.pre-uv-<timestamp>` before running `uv sync`. Re-running retries the migration on failure.
3. **Leave** backups on disk; no auto-cleanup.

### Step 12: Update owned docs and debug helpers (`reigh-worker/README.md`, `reigh-worker/STRUCTURE.md`, `reigh-worker/debug/README.md`, `reigh-worker/docs/KIJAI_SVI_IMPLEMENTATION.md`, `reigh-worker-orchestrator/scripts/debug/README.md`, `reigh-app/scripts/debug/commands/pod.py`, `docs/structure_detail/task_worker_lifecycle.md`, `reigh-app/docs/structure_detail/task_worker_lifecycle.md`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Rewrite** the worker quick-start in `README.md` for the uv flow; pin `--python 3.10` in every example; name Ubuntu 22.04/Debian 12 baseline + deadsnakes note for Ubuntu 24.04+.
2. **Update** `STRUCTURE.md` with (a) the uv flow and (b) a "Vendored Wan2GP subtree drift" note.
3. **Update** `debug/README.md`, `KIJAI_SVI_IMPLEMENTATION.md`, `scripts/debug/README.md`, `pod.py` to remove stale pip/venv instructions.
4. **Update BOTH** copies of `task_worker_lifecycle.md`.
5. **Do not touch** vendored Wan2GP upstream docs.

### Step 13: Rollback plan
**Scope:** Small
1. **Runtime rollback during first migration:** rename `venv.pre-uv-<timestamp>` back into place.
2. **No pip fallback at runtime.**
3. **Release rollback:** reverting commits restores the pip/venv flow via still-committed `requirements.txt`.

## Execution Order
1. **Phase 1 first** — port `startup_phase` writes with concrete `curl --fail`/`%{http_code}` strict helper + retry-then-exit, then cut imports over. No uv changes yet; no orchestrator control-loop changes.
2. **Phase 2** — land `pyproject.toml` + `uv.lock`, then run the cold-sync benchmark (Step 6 sub-step 3) so Phase 3 Step 9's conditional cap bump has measured data.
3. **Phase 3** — update client command builders (after grep audit) and orchestrator startup template in the same release window. Step 8 extends `useWorkerLaunchConfig.ts` for `workerRepoPath`.
4. **Phase 4** — migration sentinel (already inside Steps 7/9), then docs, then rollback drill.

## Validation Order
1. **Cheapest first:** `uv lock --python 3.10` resolution; `commandUtils.test.ts` asserting idempotent clone + cd prefix (with literal `cd /d` for cmd) + explicit `uv.exe` for cmd + apt-cache precheck for Linux; `test_startup_script.py` asserting phase sequence + strict retry with `%{http_code}` check.
2. **Fresh-install smoke tests (mandatory, Phase 3 — not deferred):**
   - Linux local (Ubuntu 22.04) cuda124.
   - Windows PowerShell cuda124.
   - Windows PowerShell cuda128.
   - **Windows cmd cuda124** — verify `cd /d` is present and the very next command after the installer uses `%USERPROFILE%\.local\bin\uv.exe` directly.
   - RunPod cuda124 — assert `.venv/pyvenv.cfg` interpreter is 3.10.
3. **Wrong-cwd AND fresh-machine acceptance smoke tests (BOTH mandatory, gating Phase 3):**
   - **(a) Wrong-cwd:** Repo already cloned at `<workerRepoPath>`. Open a fresh terminal in the user's home directory (`C:\Users\<name>` on Windows, `$HOME` on Linux). Copy-paste the generated install command. Must succeed end-to-end. Repeat for the run command. Repeat specifically for Windows cmd (`cd` vs `cd /d` is a drive-letter-boundary failure mode).
   - **(b) Fresh machine:** `<workerRepoPath>` does NOT exist yet. Open a fresh terminal in the user's home directory. Copy-paste the generated install command. Must clone the repo into `<workerRepoPath>` and then succeed end-to-end. Repeat for all three shells.
   - If either (a) or (b) fails, the migration has not solved the original transcript bug and is a no-go.
4. **Upgrade smoke tests:**
   - Linux local upgrade from existing `venv/`.
   - Linux local upgrade from existing `.venv/`.
   - Windows PowerShell upgrade from existing `venv/`.
   - Windows cmd upgrade from existing `venv/`.
   - **RunPod upgrade from existing `venv/` on mounted workspace** — post-`uv sync` interpreter is 3.10; `onnxruntime-gpu` and `insightface` still import.
5. **macOS guardrail:** `GenerationTokenPanel` still shows the unsupported message and never calls the helpers; direct mac invocation throws.
6. **Failure paths:**
   - `uv` missing but installer reachable.
   - Network down during `uv sync` after a prior successful migration → `uv sync` exits non-zero; pod visibly fails.
   - **First `deps_installing` PATCH unreachable** → pod retries 3× then `exit 1`; RunPod console shows failure.
   - **Supabase returns HTTP 500 on first PATCH** → the `%{http_code}` check catches it (unlike a bare `curl ... || return 1`), retry loop fires, `exit 1` on exhaustion.
   - Corrupted `uv.lock` stops startup with actionable logs.
   - **Ubuntu 24.04 with no deadsnakes** → `apt-cache show python3.10-venv` precheck fires, prints the deadsnakes message, exits before the generic apt failure.

## Watch Items
- **PATH propagation, cwd, AND missing-clone are THREE separate failure modes** — all must be eliminated. The Windows cmd explicit-`uv.exe`-path fix eliminates PATH propagation; the `cd /d` prefix eliminates cwd drift across drive letters; the idempotent `git clone` prefix eliminates fresh-machine missing-directory failure. **The whole migration's success criterion is that a non-technical Windows user who opens `cmd.exe` in `C:\Users\<name>` (their home directory, NOT the repo) — whether or not they have already cloned — can copy-paste the generated command and have it work end-to-end.** The wrong-cwd + fresh-machine smoke tests in Validation Order are the gating acceptance tests.
- **`curl --fail` alone is insufficient** — the plan explicitly uses `--write-out '%{http_code}'` + case match because `--fail` has inconsistent behavior across curl versions for error-body responses. Step 2's code block is the contract.
- **Step 6's benchmark is the debt-closer for FLAG-013** — if cold `uv sync` p95 > 20 min, Step 9 MUST bump `max_startup_phase_sec` to 2700.

  {
    "id": "callers-1",
    "concern": "Find the callers of the changed function. What arguments do they actually pass? Does the fix handle all of them?: I verified the direct caller chain for the new repo-path field: `useWorkerLaunchConfig.ts` owns the persisted values, `SettingsModal.tsx` consumes that hook, `GenerationSection.tsx` passes `launchConfig` and `launchSetters`, and `GenerationTokenPanel.tsx` constructs the `CommandConfig` passed to `commandUtils`. Because the plan omits `useWorkerLaunchConfig.ts` and `GenerationSection.tsx`, it does not yet fully account for the actual callers that must supply the new argument.",
    "status": "addressed",
    "severity": "significant"
  },
  {
    "id": "callers-2",
    "concern": "Find the callers of the changed function. What arguments do they actually pass? Does the fix handle all of them?: The effective caller set for the install command includes users with no local clone yet, because the current product treats 'Install' as first-time bootstrap and today's install command begins with `git clone`. The revised plan's new install-command contract does not handle that caller case because it assumes `<WORKER_REPO_PATH>` already exists before the first command runs.",
    "status": "addressed",
    "severity": "significant"
  },
  {
    "id": "scope",
    "concern": "Search for related code that handles the same concept. Is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: The broader problem is not just command text inside `commandUtils.ts`; it is the entire persisted-launch-config pipeline. Adding `workerRepoPath` changes the shape owned by `useWorkerLaunchConfig.ts`, the prop types imported in `types.ts`, the `GenerationSection.tsx` pass-through, and the `GenerationTokenPanel.tsx` command-builder call site, so the repo-path amendment is a wider state-plumbing change than the Step 8 file list suggests.",
    "status": "addressed",
    "severity": "significant"
  },
  {
    "id": "FLAG-012",
    "concern": "Startup phase HTTP handling: Recurring debt: Step 2 still does not specify a concrete HTTP-failure mechanism such as `curl --fail` or explicit status-code checking, so Supabase 4xx/5xx responses can still bypass the strict retry-and-exit path.",
    "status": "addressed",
    "severity": "significant"
  },
  {
    "id": "FLAG-013",
    "concern": "Startup phase timeout: Recurring debt: later phase writes remain lenient, so a healthy pod can still stay labeled `deps_installing` and be subject to the 30-minute startup cap without a benchmark or timeout adjustment proving that is safe for cold `uv sync` runs.",
    "status": "addressed",
    "severity": "significant"
  },
  {
    "id": "FLAG-014",
    "concern": "Linux bootstrap prerequisites: Recurring debt: the generated Linux install path is still documentation-only for distros without `python3.10-venv`, so users who skip the docs still hit a generic apt failure instead of an actionable precheck.",
    "status": "addressed",
    "severity": "significant"
  },
  {
    "id": "FLAG-015",
    "concern": "Install bootstrap: the revised first-time install commands now `cd` into `workerRepoPath` before ensuring the repo exists, so a fresh user can fail immediately on a missing directory and never reach the clone/bootstrap steps.",
    "status": "addressed",
    "severity": "significant"
  }
]

  "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-007",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: for ordinary pairs, the new diagnosis is not well-supported by the repo. the current sync effect in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l62) already syncs `url` and `primaryvariantid`, and the regenerate path reads exactly `starturl`, `endurl`, `startgenid`, `endgenid`, `startvariantid`, and `endvariantid` from `segmentslotmode.pairdata` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts#l438). adding `thumburl` and `generationid` to the sync does not, by itself, explain why an old image url is still being used for regular pairs.",

      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-018",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the merge pseudocode does not actually sync 'all image fields' as claimed. it omits `id` and `position` from change detection, and when `fresh.startimage` or `fresh.endimage` becomes `null` it preserves the stale previous object instead of syncing that null state. as written, it also needs explicit null-guards around spreads of `prev.startimage` / `prev.endimage` to be safe in strict typescript.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-106",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan's claim that `worker_state.py:200-204` (the 30-minute startup safety net) catches abandoned pods that exited before patching is slightly imprecise. line 200-204 only runs when `in_startup_phase` is true, which requires `startup_phase` to be in `('deps_installing', 'deps_verified', 'worker_starting')` per line 161. a pod that exits before its first patch has `startup_phase = none`, so `in_startup_phase` is false and the code falls through to the normal timeout checks (active_stale / not_claiming) at line 207+, not the 30-minute startup cap. both paths eventually reap the worker, so the end result is the same, but the overview's reasoning (naming 200-204 specifically) is wrong. worth correcting so future readers don't mis-reason about the invariant.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-107",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked worker_state.py line 161 (`in_startup_phase = startup_phase in (...)`) against the plan's assumption that subsequent lenient writes are safe. if deps_installing succeeds but deps_verified silently fails, startup_phase stays at 'deps_installing' and the 30-minute cap from line 200-204 applies \u2014 even though the pod is actually progressing through `uv sync`. on a slow runpod image with cold wheel downloads, `uv sync --extra cuda124` plus wan2gp deps can take 20+ minutes. there's no concrete benchmark in the plan that says the 30-minute cap is safely above the expected sync time; if `uv sync` legitimately takes 25 minutes and the deps_verified patch fails on a transient network blip, the orchestrator will terminate a healthy pod. plan should either bump the cap or note that deps_verified swallowing is acceptable because the cap is generously above typical sync time.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-109",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the step 2 retry loop uses a naked `sleep 2` between attempts, which adds up to 6 seconds of latency on the happy path when the first patch eventually succeeds on attempt 2 or 3. for pods where supabase is reachable but slow during the initial moments of a cold runpod start, the retry logic is fine. however, if the first attempt fails with a tls handshake delay and the retry loop sleeps 2s per attempt regardless of whether curl itself has been blocking for tens of seconds, the total startup latency penalty could be substantial. this is a minor tuning concern, not a correctness issue.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    }
  ],

        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: for ordinary pairs, the new diagnosis is not well-supported by the repo. the current sync effect in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l62) already syncs `url` and `primaryvariantid`, and the regenerate path reads exactly `starturl`, `endurl`, `startgenid`, `endgenid`, `startvariantid`, and `endvariantid` from `segmentslotmode.pairdata` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts#l438). adding `thumburl` and `generationid` to the sync does not, by itself, explain why an old image url is still being used for regular pairs.",

        "id": "DEBT-018",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the merge pseudocode does not actually sync 'all image fields' as claimed. it omits `id` and `position` from change detection, and when `fresh.startimage` or `fresh.endimage` becomes `null` it preserves the stale previous object instead of syncing that null state. as written, it also needs explicit null-guards around spreads of `prev.startimage` / `prev.endimage` to be safe in strict typescript.",

      {
        "id": "DEBT-106",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan's claim that `worker_state.py:200-204` (the 30-minute startup safety net) catches abandoned pods that exited before patching is slightly imprecise. line 200-204 only runs when `in_startup_phase` is true, which requires `startup_phase` to be in `('deps_installing', 'deps_verified', 'worker_starting')` per line 161. a pod that exits before its first patch has `startup_phase = none`, so `in_startup_phase` is false and the code falls through to the normal timeout checks (active_stale / not_claiming) at line 207+, not the 30-minute startup cap. both paths eventually reap the worker, so the end result is the same, but the overview's reasoning (naming 200-204 specifically) is wrong. worth correcting so future readers don't mis-reason about the invariant.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-107",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked worker_state.py line 161 (`in_startup_phase = startup_phase in (...)`) against the plan's assumption that subsequent lenient writes are safe. if deps_installing succeeds but deps_verified silently fails, startup_phase stays at 'deps_installing' and the 30-minute cap from line 200-204 applies \u2014 even though the pod is actually progressing through `uv sync`. on a slow runpod image with cold wheel downloads, `uv sync --extra cuda124` plus wan2gp deps can take 20+ minutes. there's no concrete benchmark in the plan that says the 30-minute cap is safely above the expected sync time; if `uv sync` legitimately takes 25 minutes and the deps_verified patch fails on a transient network blip, the orchestrator will terminate a healthy pod. plan should either bump the cap or note that deps_verified swallowing is acceptable because the cap is generously above typical sync time.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
        ]
      }

Read this file first — it contains 5 checks, each with a question and guidance.
For each check, investigate the codebase, then add your findings to the `findings` array for that check.

Each finding needs:
- "detail": what you specifically checked and what you found (at least a full sentence)
- "flagged": true if this describes a difference, risk, or tension — even if you think it's justified. false only if purely informational with no possible downside.

When in doubt, flag it — the gate can accept tradeoffs, but it can't act on findings it never sees.

Good: {"detail": "Checked callers of nthroot_mod in solveset.py line 1205 — passes prime moduli only, consistent with the fix.", "flagged": false}
Good: {"detail": "The fix handles empty tuples but not single-element tuples which need a trailing comma.", "flagged": true}
Bad: {"detail": "No issue found", "flagged": false}  ← too brief, will be rejected
Bad: {"detail": "The hints suggest approach X but the plan uses Y. However Y is consistent with X's intent.", "flagged": false}  ← a different approach than the hints IS a flag. You found a divergence — flag it. The gate decides if it's acceptable.

After filling in checks, add any additional concerns to the `flags` array (e.g., security, performance, dependencies).
Use the standard format (id, concern, category, severity_hint, evidence). This array can be empty.

Workflow: read the file → investigate → read file again → add finding → write file back. Repeat for each check.

This is critique iteration 5. The template file includes prior findings with their status. Verify addressed flags were actually fixed, re-flag if inadequate, and check for new issues introduced by the revision.

        - Robustness level: standard. Use balanced judgment. Flag significant risks, but do not spend flags on minor polish or executor-obvious boilerplate.

        - `verified_flag_ids`: list flag IDs from prior iterations that the revised plan actually resolves (e.g., if the plan was revised to fix FLAG-001, and you confirm the fix is correct, include "FLAG-001"). Only include flags you've verified — don't guess.

        Idea:
Migrate the Python worker from pip/venv to uv with a lockfile, running `uv sync` unconditionally on every launch. Scope spans THREE components: (1) the Python worker repo (add pyproject.toml + uv.lock from existing requirements.txt; no code changes inside the worker), (2) the client's Settings modal where install/run command strings live (likely commandUtils.ts), and (3) the orchestrator that spawns workers using those same command strings. Key questions the plan must answer: where the install/run command strings are actually defined and whether client and orchestrator share that code or duplicate it; how `uv` itself gets onto end-user machines (bundle / detect-and-download / one-time install instruction); migration path for existing users with stale pre-uv .venv directories; cross-platform behavior on Linux/macOS/Windows; lockfile generation matching current resolved versions; rollback story if `uv sync` fails (network down, corrupted lockfile); and the Settings modal UX after migration (does the manual install button become a 'force resync' button?). Deliverables: (1) every file to touch across worker/client/orchestrator, (2) decision on uv bootstrapping with justification, (3) migration path for existing users, (4) test plan covering Linux/macOS/Windows + fresh-install + upgrade-from-pip-venv, (5) rollback plan.

User notes and answers:
- EXECUTE_APPROVAL BLOCKED — required amendment to T7/Step 7 before user approves.

PROBLEM: I read plan_v4.md Step 7 carefully and the generated install/run commands DO NOT bundle a 'cd' into the worker repo directory. Every command (Linux install, Linux run, Windows PowerShell install/run, Windows cmd install/run) implicitly assumes the user is already cwd'd into the worker repo. That assumption is exactly what failed in the original support transcript that motivated this whole migration: a Windows user opened cmd.exe in C:\Users\MC, copy-pasted 'pip install -r requirements.txt', and got 'No such file or directory' because requirements.txt wasn't in their cwd. After this migration, the same user pasting the new uv command from the same cwd would get 'error: No pyproject.toml found in current directory or any parent directory'. Same bug, one layer deeper. The plan currently fixes Windows PATH propagation (good — explicit %USERPROFILE%\.local\bin\uv.exe path) but does NOT fix the cwd problem, which is the actual transcript failure.

REQUIRED AMENDMENT to T7 / Step 7 of the plan, plus matching test updates in T7 sub-step 4:

(1) Every generated install/run command MUST start with a directory change into the worker repo, before any uv invocation. Concrete shapes:
   - Linux install/run: prepend 'cd "<WORKER_REPO_PATH>" && ' to the entire command.
   - Windows PowerShell install/run: prepend 'Set-Location -LiteralPath "<WORKER_REPO_PATH>"; ' (or the cmd equivalent if generating .bat).
   - Windows cmd install/run: prepend 'cd /d "<WORKER_REPO_PATH>" && ' (the /d flag is mandatory so it works across drive letters).
   The cd must come BEFORE the uv installer step on first install too, because the installer is fine to run from any cwd but the subsequent 'uv sync' must run inside the repo.

(2) Where does <WORKER_REPO_PATH> come from? The plan must pick ONE of these and document the choice in Step 7:
   (a) Read it from an existing Settings-modal config field that already stores the worker-repo path. If such a field exists today, use it; the generated command bakes in the absolute path the user already configured. This is the cleanest option if the field exists — investigate first.
   (b) If no such field exists, ADD one to the Settings modal in Step 8 (UX copy step, currently small): a 'Worker repo location' text input with a default of '%USERPROFILE%\Reigh-Worker' on Windows and '/Users/user_c042661f/reigh-worker' on Linux, persisted to the same store the rest of Settings uses, and threaded into commandUtils.ts. Step 8 scope grows from Small to Medium to accommodate this.
   (c) Hard-code a standardized path ('%USERPROFILE%\Reigh-Worker' on Windows, '/Users/user_c042661f/reigh-worker' on Linux) and document that users MUST clone to that exact path. Simplest but hostile to users who already cloned elsewhere — only acceptable if existing users are rare or the install instructions also include the git clone command targeting that path.

The plan revision must investigate (a) first by grepping reigh-app/src/shared/components/SettingsModal/ for existing repo-path state. If a path field exists, use it. If not, default to (b). Reject (c) unless (a) and (b) are both infeasible.

(3) commandUtils.test.ts updates required by T7 sub-step 4: every test case (Linux install, Linux run, Windows PowerShell install, Windows PowerShell run, Windows cmd install, Windows cmd run) MUST assert that the generated command starts with the appropriate cd-equivalent and contains the configured/passed worker-repo path. The Windows cmd cases must specifically assert 'cd /d' (with the /d flag) so future regressions where someone drops the /d are caught.

(4) Add an explicit acceptance criterion to Step 7 (and to the validation/smoke test list in 'Validation Order'): 'Smoke test: open a fresh terminal in a directory OTHER than the worker repo (e.g. user home), copy-paste the generated command, and verify it succeeds. If it fails because the cwd was wrong, the migration has not solved the original transcript bug.' This must be one of the Phase 3 smoke tests, not deferred to Phase 4.

(5) Watch item to add: 'The whole point of this migration is that a non-technical Windows user who opens cmd.exe in their home directory must be able to run the generated command and have it work. PATH propagation and cwd are TWO separate failure modes — both must be eliminated, not just PATH.'

Please revise plan_v4 → plan_v5 incorporating this amendment, then re-critique, re-gate, re-finalize, and stop again at EXECUTE_APPROVAL. Do NOT execute.

        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: Migrate Worker Bootstrap From pip/venv to uv

## Overview
Migrate the Python worker runtime from pip/venv to `uv` with a committed lockfile, and run `uv sync --locked` on every launch across the client Settings modal, the orchestrator RunPod startup template, and the worker repo itself.

Current shape verified in the repo:
- [reigh-worker/pyproject.toml](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker/pyproject.toml) exists but is not the runtime source of truth; installs come from [requirements.txt](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker/requirements.txt) and the vendored [Wan2GP/requirements.txt](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker/Wan2GP/requirements.txt).
- Client install commands in [commandUtils.ts:53,66](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/shared/components/SettingsModal/commandUtils.ts) currently begin with `git clone --depth 1 https://github.com/banodoco/Reigh-Worker.git` then `cd Reigh-Worker`.
- `CommandConfig` is defined in [reigh-app/src/shared/components/SettingsModal/types.ts:13](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/shared/components/SettingsModal/types.ts) with `gpuType: string` at line 15. `commandUtils.ts:42` already branches on `gpuType === "nvidia-50"` to select cuda128 vs cuda124 — **the new `uv sync --extra` must preserve this branch**, not hard-code `cuda124`.
- The persisted launch-config seam is [useWorkerLaunchConfig.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/shared/components/SettingsModal/hooks/useWorkerLaunchConfig.ts), which today owns 6 fields via `usePersistentState`. `SettingsModal.tsx` is NOT the persistence seam. [sections/GenerationSection/GenerationSection.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/shared/components/SettingsModal/sections/GenerationSection/GenerationSection.tsx) forwards `launchConfig`/`launchSetters` into `GenerationTokenPanel`, which constructs the `CommandConfig`.
- Both the live [runpod_client.py:1336](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker-orchestrator/gpu_orchestrator/runpod_client.py) and the template-backed [worker_startup.template.sh:328](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker-orchestrator/gpu_orchestrator/runpod/worker_startup.template.sh) launch `python worker.py` directly.
- **RunPod actually runs Python 3.10.** Base image is `runpod/pytorch:2.4.0-py3.11-...` ([runpod_client.py:357](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker-orchestrator/gpu_orchestrator/runpod_client.py)), but both `runpod_client.py:985/993` and `worker_startup.template.sh:105/113` apt-install `python3.10-venv` and create the runtime venv with `python3.10 -m venv`.
- [runpod_client.py:890-922](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker-orchestrator/gpu_orchestrator/runpod_client.py) defines `update_worker_phase`, which silently swallows all errors.
- [worker_state.py:140](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker-orchestrator/gpu_orchestrator/worker_state.py) reads `startup_phase` from an in-memory dict populated by Supabase polling. There is NO pod-file reader in the orchestrator control loop.
- `getInstallationCommand`/`getRunCommand` are referenced in four files: `commandUtils.ts`, `commandUtils.test.ts`, `GenerationTokenPanel.tsx`, `GenerationTokenPanel.test.tsx`. `GenerationTokenPanel` is the only non-test caller and already guards `computerType === "mac"`.
- Legacy `runpod_client` references live in [scripts/debug/commands/storage.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker-orchestrator/scripts/debug/commands/storage.py), [gpu_orchestrator/logging_config.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker-orchestrator/gpu_orchestrator/logging_config.py), [api_orchestrator/logging_config.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker-orchestrator/api_orchestrator/logging_config.py).
- `task_worker_lifecycle.md` is duplicated under both [docs/structure_detail/](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/docs/structure_detail/task_worker_lifecycle.md) and [reigh-app/docs/structure_detail/](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/docs/structure_detail/task_worker_lifecycle.md).

### Scope guardrails (pinned decisions)
- **Python matrix:** Linux local = 3.10, Windows local = 3.10, RunPod = 3.10. `pyproject.toml` declares `requires-python = ">=3.10,<3.11"`. Every `uv sync`, `uv run`, and `uv lock` pins `--python 3.10` explicitly.
- **GPU extras branch on `gpuType`:** `commandUtils.ts` must select `--extra cuda128` when `gpuType === "nvidia-50"` and `--extra cuda124` otherwise — identical branching to today's torch install (`commandUtils.ts:42`). This preserves current Windows 50-series behavior. Linux/RunPod stays on `cuda124` because only Windows exposes the 50-series option today.
- **Linux distro scope + precheck:** Supported baseline is Ubuntu 22.04 LTS / Debian 12. The generated Linux install command includes an inline apt-cache precheck so Ubuntu 24.04+ users hit an actionable deadsnakes message.
- **Process model:** orchestrator template keeps `python worker.py` (wrapped as `uv run --python 3.10 python worker.py ...`).
- **Canonical bootstrap:** Reigh-owned `reigh-worker/pyproject.toml` + `uv.lock` is the single source of truth. Vendored `Wan2GP/scripts/install.*` and `setup.py` out of scope. Wan2GP subtree drift documented in `reigh-worker/STRUCTURE.md` as accepted debt.
- **macOS contract:** throw `UnsupportedPlatformError` for mac. `NonMacComputerType` narrow type is accepted debt.
- **Worker-repo path:** a new persisted `workerRepoPath` field is added to `WorkerLaunchConfigValues`/`WorkerLaunchConfigSetters` in `useWorkerLaunchConfig.ts`, **AND a matching `workerRepoPath: string` field is added to `CommandConfig` in `types.ts`.** Defaults: `%USERPROFILE%\Reigh-Worker` (Windows), `$HOME/reigh-worker` (Linux).
- **Install commands begin with idempotent `git clone` then cd. Run commands begin with cd then the sentinel preguard for venv backup, then `git pull`/sync/run.** Run is explicitly an upgrade path and must NOT bypass the pre-uv migration.
- **Phase signaling (clarified vs. prior draft):** Phase 1 hardens only the template side. The strict `update_worker_phase_strict` helper uses explicit `curl --write-out '%{http_code}'` parsing with a `case '2*')` match — **NOT `curl --fail`**, because `--fail` has inconsistent version behavior on error bodies. First `deps_installing` PATCH wraps in 3-attempt retry + `exit 1`. Subsequent phase writes stay lenient.
- **Orchestrator control-loop policy (clarified):** There are **no unconditional** orchestrator control-loop changes. **The ONE permitted change** is a conditional bump of `max_startup_phase_sec` at `worker_state.py:200` from 1800 to 2700, **gated on Step 6's benchmark result** (only if cold-cache `uv sync` p95 exceeds 20 minutes). Otherwise `worker_state.py` is untouched. This is the only edit to the orchestrator control loop permitted by this migration.

## Phase 1: Preserve Startup-Phase Invariant In the Template Path

### Step 1: Document the startup_phase contract (`gpu_orchestrator/runpod_client.py`, `gpu_orchestrator/worker_state.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Enumerate** every `metadata.startup_phase` value the live orchestrator writes and reads across `runpod_client.py:890-922` and `worker_state.py:140,161,201`.
2. **Audit** the four `|| return 0` / `|| true` swallow points in `update_worker_phase`.

### Step 2: Port startup_phase writes into the template with explicit `%{http_code}` retry-then-exit on first PATCH (`gpu_orchestrator/runpod/worker_startup.template.sh`, `gpu_orchestrator/runpod/startup_script.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Port** the `update_worker_phase` shell helper into `worker_startup.template.sh` at identical transition points. Keep pip/venv install steps intact.
2. **Define `update_worker_phase_strict`** with concrete curl flags — **NOT `--fail`**:
   ```sh
   update_worker_phase_strict() {
       local phase="$1"
       local key="$SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY"
       [ -z "$key" ] && return 1  # missing key is a hard failure, not silent
       local http_code
       http_code=$(curl --silent --show-error --output /dev/null \
           --write-out '%{http_code}' \
           --max-time 10 \
           --retry 0 \
           -X PATCH \
           "${SUPABASE_URL}/rest/v1/workers?id=eq.${WORKER_ID}" \
           -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
           -H "Authorization: Bearer $key" \
           -H "apikey: $key" \
           -H "Prefer: return=minimal" \
           -d "{\"metadata\":{\"startup_phase\":\"$phase\"}}") || return 1
       case "$http_code" in
           2*) return 0 ;;
           *) echo "update_worker_phase_strict: HTTP $http_code" >&2 ; return 1 ;;
       esac
   }
   ```
   Rationale: `curl --fail` has inconsistent cross-version behavior on error bodies; explicit `%{http_code}` + case match is cleaner and testable.
3. **Wrap the first `deps_installing` PATCH in bounded retry + hard exit**:
   ```sh
   for attempt in 1 2 3; do
       if update_worker_phase_strict "deps_installing"; then break; fi
       if [ "$attempt" = "3" ]; then
           echo "❌ FATAL: deps_installing PATCH failed 3× (network, auth, or Supabase 5xx); aborting so RunPod console surfaces the failure" >&2
           exit 1
       fi
       sleep 2
   done
   ```
4. Subsequent phase writes (`deps_verified`, `worker_starting`, `ready`) retain the legacy lenient helper.
5. **Rationale:** if the first PATCH fails permanently, the pod `exit 1`s before `uv sync`; `worker_state.py:200-204`'s existing 30-minute safety net reaps the abandoned worker.
6. **Parameterize** the phase-update target in `startup_script.py` if the rendered template needs an endpoint/token.

### Step 3: Template-side parity tests only (`tests/gpu_orchestrator/runpod/test_startup_script.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Extend** `test_startup_script.py` to assert: (a) phases written at correct sequence points; (b) strict helper uses `--write-out '%{http_code}'` and `case '2*')`; (c) first `deps_installing` has 3-attempt retry + `exit 1`; (d) subsequent phases remain lenient.
2. **Do NOT modify** `tests/gpu_orchestrator/control/phases/test_state.py` or `test_periodic.py`.

### Step 4: Cut active callers over to `gpu_orchestrator.runpod` (`gpu_orchestrator/control_loop.py`, `scripts/spawn_gpu.py`, `scripts/shutdown_all_workers.py`, `scripts/ssh_to_worker.py`, `scripts/terminate_single_worker.py`, `scripts/debug/services/workers.py`, `scripts/debug/commands/storage.py`, `gpu_orchestrator/logging_config.py`, `api_orchestrator/logging_config.py`, `tests/test_runpod_decomposition.py`, `tests/scripts/test_debug_services.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Switch** imports from `gpu_orchestrator.runpod_client` to `gpu_orchestrator.runpod`.
2. **Update** both logging allowlists.
3. **Leave** `runpod_client.py` as an unreferenced compatibility shim.
4. **Run** the full orchestrator test suite.

## Phase 2: Worker Repo Becomes a Real uv Project

### Step 5: Pin the Python interpreter and dependency graph (`reigh-worker/pyproject.toml`)
**Scope:** Large
1. **Expand** `pyproject.toml` into a full PEP 621 project. Set `requires-python = ">=3.10,<3.11"`.
2. **Translate** dependencies from `requirements.txt` and `Wan2GP/requirements.txt` into `pyproject.toml`. Preserve platform-specific wheel URLs via `[tool.uv.sources]` / environment markers.
3. **Model** the torch split as extras: `cuda124` (Linux, RunPod, non-50-series Windows) and `cuda128` (Windows NVIDIA 50-series).
4. **Preserve** existing pytest config and entrypoints verbatim.

### Step 6: Generate, validate, and benchmark `uv.lock` (`reigh-worker/uv.lock`, `reigh-worker/requirements.txt`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Generate** `uv.lock` via `uv lock --python 3.10`.
2. **Validate** resolution: `uv sync --locked --python 3.10 --extra cuda124` AND `uv sync --locked --python 3.10 --extra cuda128` in throwaway venvs. Diff against current pip-based installs; pin drift in `pyproject.toml`.
3. **Benchmark cold-cache wall time** on a fresh RunPod pod with no torch cache. Time `uv sync --locked --python 3.10 --extra cuda124`. Record p50 and approximate p95 across 3 runs. Record the result in the PR description. **If p95 > 20 minutes, Step 9 MUST bump `max_startup_phase_sec` in `gpu_orchestrator/worker_state.py:200` from 1800 to 2700.** Otherwise leave unchanged.
4. **Keep** `requirements.txt` committed during rollout as rollback ballast.

## Phase 3: Command Surfaces Switch To `uv`

### Step 7: Rewrite client command builders with `git clone` + cd + uv, preserving gpuType branching (`reigh-app/src/shared/components/SettingsModal/commandUtils.ts`, `reigh-app/src/shared/components/SettingsModal/types.ts`, `reigh-app/src/shared/components/SettingsModal/commandUtils.test.ts`)
**Scope:** Large
1. **Grep audit (first action):** re-run `rg "getInstallationCommand|getRunCommand"` across `reigh-app/src/`. Confirm only `GenerationTokenPanel.tsx` + the two test files call them.
2. **Extend `CommandConfig` in `types.ts:13`** to carry `workerRepoPath: string` (required). This is the import seam — `commandUtils.ts` imports `CommandConfig` from this file, so the type edit lives here, not in `commandUtils.ts`.
3. **Preserve gpuType branching:** every install/run command must select `--extra` dynamically via `const extra = gpuType === "nvidia-50" ? "cuda128" : "cuda124";` then interpolate `--extra ${extra}`. No hard-coded `cuda124`. This matches today's `commandUtils.ts:42` behavior and keeps the Windows 50-series path working.
4. **Install commands start with idempotent git clone, then cd, then the rest.** **Run commands start with cd, then the sentinel preguard for venv backup (identical idempotent logic to install), then `git pull` → `uv sync` → `uv run`** — so upgrade users who copy-paste Run still migrate stale pre-uv envs.
5. **Concrete shapes** (all use `${extra}` where shown):
   - **Linux install:**
     ```sh
     [ -d "<workerRepoPath>/.git" ] || git clone --depth 1 https://github.com/banodoco/Reigh-Worker.git "<workerRepoPath>" && \
     cd "<workerRepoPath>" && \
     apt-cache show python3.10-venv >/dev/null 2>&1 || { echo "ERROR: python3.10-venv not in apt; install deadsnakes PPA first — see README"; exit 1; } && \
     sudo apt-get update && sudo apt-get install -y python3.10-venv python3.10-dev ffmpeg && \
     curl -LsSf https://astral.sh/uv/install.sh | sh && \
     source $HOME/.local/bin/env && \
     if [ ! -f .uv-migrated ]; then { [ -d venv ] && mv venv "venv.pre-uv-$(date +%s)" || true; } && { [ -d .venv ] && mv .venv ".venv.pre-uv-$(date +%s)" || true; }; fi && \
     uv sync --locked --python 3.10 --extra ${extra} && \
     touch .uv-migrated && \
     uv run --python 3.10 python run_worker.py ...
     ```
   - **Linux run (now includes sentinel preguard + venv backup):**
     ```sh
     cd "<workerRepoPath>" && \
     if [ ! -f .uv-migrated ]; then { [ -d venv ] && mv venv "venv.pre-uv-$(date +%s)" || true; } && { [ -d .venv ] && mv .venv ".venv.pre-uv-$(date +%s)" || true; }; fi && \
     git pull && \
     uv sync --locked --python 3.10 --extra ${extra} && \
     touch .uv-migrated && \
     uv run --python 3.10 python run_worker.py ...
     ```
   - **Windows PowerShell install:**
     ```powershell
     if (-not (Test-Path "<workerRepoPath>\.git")) { git clone --depth 1 https://github.com/banodoco/Reigh-Worker.git "<workerRepoPath>" }
     Set-Location -LiteralPath "<workerRepoPath>"
     powershell -ExecutionPolicy ByPass -c "irm https://astral.sh/uv/install.ps1 | iex"
     $env:Path = [System.Environment]::GetEnvironmentVariable('Path','User') + ';' + $env:Path
     if (-not (Test-Path .uv-migrated)) { if (Test-Path venv) { Rename-Item venv "venv.pre-uv-$(Get-Date -UFormat %s)" }; if (Test-Path .venv) { Rename-Item .venv ".venv.pre-uv-$(Get-Date -UFormat %s)" } }
     uv sync --locked --python 3.10 --extra ${extra}
     New-Item -ItemType File -Path .uv-migrated -Force | Out-Null
     uv run --python 3.10 python run_worker.py ...
     ```
   - **Windows PowerShell run (now includes sentinel preguard):**
     ```powershell
     Set-Location -LiteralPath "<workerRepoPath>"
     if (-not (Test-Path .uv-migrated)) { if (Test-Path venv) { Rename-Item venv "venv.pre-uv-$(Get-Date -UFormat %s)" }; if (Test-Path .venv) { Rename-Item .venv ".venv.pre-uv-$(Get-Date -UFormat %s)" } }
     git pull
     uv sync --locked --python 3.10 --extra ${extra}
     New-Item -ItemType File -Path .uv-migrated -Force | Out-Null
     uv run --python 3.10 python run_worker.py ...
     ```
   - **Windows cmd install:**
     ```bat
     if not exist "<workerRepoPath>\.git" git clone --depth 1 https://github.com/banodoco/Reigh-Worker.git "<workerRepoPath>"
     cd /d "<workerRepoPath>"
     powershell -ExecutionPolicy ByPass -c "irm https://astral.sh/uv/install.ps1 | iex"
     if not exist .uv-migrated ( if exist venv ren venv venv.pre-uv-%RANDOM% ) & ( if exist .venv ren .venv .venv.pre-uv-%RANDOM% )
     "%USERPROFILE%\.local\bin\uv.exe" sync --locked --python 3.10 --extra ${extra}
     type nul > .uv-migrated
     "%USERPROFILE%\.local\bin\uv.exe" run --python 3.10 python run_worker.py ...
     ```
     Note: `cd /d` is MANDATORY (cross-drive-letter); `%USERPROFILE%\.local\bin\uv.exe` is used explicitly so no PATH propagation is needed.
   - **Windows cmd run (now includes sentinel preguard):**
     ```bat
     cd /d "<workerRepoPath>"
     if not exist .uv-migrated ( if exist venv ren venv venv.pre-uv-%RANDOM% ) & ( if exist .venv ren .venv .venv.pre-uv-%RANDOM% )
     git pull
     "%USERPROFILE%\.local\bin\uv.exe" sync --locked --python 3.10 --extra ${extra}
     type nul > .uv-migrated
     "%USERPROFILE%\.local\bin\uv.exe" run --python 3.10 python run_worker.py ...
     ```
6. **Change the macOS contract:** throw `UnsupportedPlatformError` for `computerType === "mac"`. Safe per sub-step 1 audit.
7. **Update** `commandUtils.test.ts`. For every non-mac case:
   - Assert install begins with the correct idempotent-clone pattern, followed by the shell-appropriate cd (cmd test asserts literal `cd /d`).
   - **Assert run begins with cd, then the sentinel preguard block, then `git pull`** — specifically assert the preguard exists for Linux (`[ ! -f .uv-migrated ]`), PowerShell (`if (-not (Test-Path .uv-migrated))`), and cmd (`if not exist .uv-migrated`). A future regression that drops the preguard is caught at test time.
   - Assert `--extra cuda124` appears when `gpuType !== "nvidia-50"` and `--extra cuda128` appears when `gpuType === "nvidia-50"`. **Cover this for Linux, Windows PowerShell, and Windows cmd install AND run**, so a future regression that drops gpuType branching is caught at test time. Fixture cases: `{ computerType: "linux", gpuType: "nvidia-30-40" }` → cuda124; `{ computerType: "windows", gpuType: "nvidia-50", windowsShell: "powershell" }` → cuda128; `{ computerType: "windows", gpuType: "nvidia-30-40", windowsShell: "cmd" }` → cuda124; `{ computerType: "windows", gpuType: "nvidia-50", windowsShell: "cmd" }` → cuda128.
   - Assert Linux install contains `apt-cache show python3.10-venv` precheck.
   - Assert Windows cmd uses `"%USERPROFILE%\.local\bin\uv.exe"` (explicit path).
   - Use fixture `workerRepoPath` values: `C:\\Users\\MC\\Reigh-Worker` (Windows), `/home/tester/reigh-worker` (Linux). Assert the path appears verbatim in clone, cd, and sentinel check.
   - mac case: asserts `throw UnsupportedPlatformError`.

## Phase 4: Migration, Docs, Rollback

### Step 11: One-time migration sentinel (implemented inside Steps 7 and 9 for BOTH install and run)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Write** `.uv-migrated` only after the first successful `uv sync`.
2. **Behavior:** on launch via EITHER the Install command OR the Run command, if the sentinel is absent, rename existing `venv/` → `venv.pre-uv-<timestamp>` and `.venv/` → `.venv.pre-uv-<timestamp>` before running `uv sync`. This must be present in the Run command shapes, not only Install, because upgrade users frequently copy-paste Run.
3. **Leave** backups on disk; no auto-cleanup.

### Step 12: Update owned docs and debug helpers (`reigh-worker/README.md`, `reigh-worker/STRUCTURE.md`, `reigh-worker/debug/README.md`, `reigh-worker/docs/KIJAI_SVI_IMPLEMENTATION.md`, `reigh-worker-orchestrator/scripts/debug/README.md`, `reigh-app/scripts/debug/commands/pod.py`, `docs/structure_detail/task_worker_lifecycle.md`, `reigh-app/docs/structure_detail/task_worker_lifecycle.md`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Rewrite** the worker quick-start in `README.md` for the uv flow; pin `--python 3.10` in every example; name Ubuntu 22.04/Debian 12 baseline + deadsnakes note for Ubuntu 24.04+.
2. **Update** `STRUCTURE.md` with (a) the uv flow and (b) a "Vendored Wan2GP subtree drift" note.
3. **Update** `debug/README.md`, `KIJAI_SVI_IMPLEMENTATION.md`, `scripts/debug/README.md`, `pod.py` to remove stale pip/venv instructions.
4. **Update BOTH** copies of `task_worker_lifecycle.md`.
5. **Do not touch** vendored Wan2GP upstream docs.

### Step 13: Rollback plan
**Scope:** Small
1. **Runtime rollback during first migration:** rename `venv.pre-uv-<timestamp>` back into place.
2. **No pip fallback at runtime.**
3. **Release rollback:** reverting commits restores the pip/venv flow via still-committed `requirements.txt`.

## Execution Order
1. **Phase 1 first** — port `startup_phase` writes with explicit `%{http_code}` strict helper + retry-then-exit, then cut imports over. No uv changes yet; no orchestrator control-loop changes.
2. **Phase 2** — land `pyproject.toml` + `uv.lock`, then run the cold-sync benchmark (Step 6 sub-step 3) so Phase 3 Step 9's conditional cap bump has measured data.
3. **Phase 3** — update client command builders (types.ts + commandUtils.ts + tests) and orchestrator startup template in the same release window. Step 8 extends `useWorkerLaunchConfig.ts` for `workerRepoPath`.
4. **Phase 4** — migration sentinel (already inside Steps 7/9 for both install and run), then docs, then rollback drill.

## Validation Order
1. **Cheapest first:** `uv lock --python 3.10` resolution; `commandUtils.test.ts` asserting idempotent clone + cd prefix (with literal `cd /d` for cmd) + explicit `uv.exe` for cmd + apt-cache precheck for Linux + **gpuType branching (cuda124 vs cuda128) across all three shells** + **run-command sentinel preguard**; `test_startup_script.py` asserting phase sequence + strict retry with `%{http_code}` check.
2. **Fresh-install smoke tests (mandatory, Phase 3):**
   - Linux local (Ubuntu 22.04) cuda124.
   - Windows PowerShell cuda124 (non-50-series GPU).
   - **Windows PowerShell cuda128 (nvidia-50)** — verifies gpuType branching emits `--extra cuda128`.
   - **Windows cmd cuda124** — verifies `cd /d` and explicit `uv.exe`.
   - **Windows cmd cuda128 (nvidia-50)** — verifies gpuType branching in the cmd branch too.
   - RunPod cuda124 — assert `.venv/pyvenv.cfg` interpreter is 3.10.
3. **Wrong-cwd + fresh-machine + run-path-migration acceptance smoke tests (ALL mandatory, gating Phase 3):**
   - **(a) Wrong-cwd (Install):** Repo already cloned at `<workerRepoPath>`. Fresh terminal in user home. Paste install command. Must succeed.
   - **(b) Fresh machine:** `<workerRepoPath>` does NOT exist. Fresh terminal in user home. Paste install command. Must clone + bootstrap end-to-end.
   - **(c) Run-path migration:** `<workerRepoPath>` exists with a stale pre-uv `venv/` inside, no `.uv-migrated` sentinel. Fresh terminal in user home. Paste **Run** command (not Install). Must rename `venv/` → `venv.pre-uv-<ts>`, sync, write sentinel, and launch. Repeat for Linux, PowerShell, and cmd.
   - If any of (a)/(b)/(c) fails, the migration has not solved the transcript bug and is a no-go.
4. **Upgrade smoke tests:**
   - Linux local upgrade from existing `venv/`.
   - Linux local upgrade from existing `.venv/`.
   - Windows PowerShell upgrade from existing `venv/`.
   - Windows cmd upgrade from existing `venv/`.
   - **RunPod upgrade from existing `venv/` on mounted workspace** — post-`uv sync` interpreter is 3.10; `onnxruntime-gpu` and `insightface` still import.
5. **macOS guardrail:** `GenerationTokenPanel` still shows the unsupported message and never calls the helpers; direct mac invocation throws.
6. **Failure paths:**
   - `uv` missing but installer reachable.
   - Network down during `uv sync` after a prior successful migration → `uv sync` exits non-zero.
   - **First `deps_installing` PATCH unreachable** → pod retries 3× then `exit 1`.
   - **Supabase returns HTTP 500 on first PATCH** → `%{http_code}` catches it, retry loop fires, `exit 1` on exhaustion.
   - Corrupted `uv.lock` stops startup with actionable logs.
   - **Ubuntu 24.04 with no deadsnakes** → `apt-cache show python3.10-venv` precheck fires.

## Watch Items
- **PATH propagation, cwd, missing-clone, AND stale-venv-on-run are FOUR separate failure modes** — all must be eliminated. Windows cmd explicit-`uv.exe`-path eliminates PATH propagation; `cd /d` eliminates cwd drift across drive letters; idempotent `git clone` eliminates fresh-machine failure; **run-command sentinel preguard eliminates stale-venv failure for upgrade users who copy-paste Run instead of Install**. The wrong-cwd + fresh-machine + run-path-migration smoke tests in Validation Order are the gating acceptance tests.
- **gpuType branching is a regression target.** Today's `commandUtils.ts:42` branches on `gpuType === "nvidia-50"` to select the cuda128 torch path. The new `--extra` value must branch identically. Tests cover both cuda124 and cuda128 per shell.
- **`curl --fail` is NOT used.** Step 2's helper uses explicit `--write-out '%{http_code}'` + `case '2*')` because `--fail` has inconsistent cross-version behavior on error bodies. Step 2's code block is the contract.
- **Step 6's benchmark is the debt-closer for the 30-min startup cap.** If cold `uv sync` p95 > 20 min, Step 9 bumps `max_startup_phase_sec` to 2700 — the ONLY orchestrator control-loop change permitted by this migration.

      "criterion": "All live orchestrator imports of `gpu_orchestrator.runpod_client` are cut over to `gpu_orchestrator.runpod`, including `control_loop.py`, the four operational scripts, `scripts/debug/services/workers.py`, `scripts/debug/commands/storage.py`, and both `logging_config.py` allowlists.",
      "priority": "must"
    },
    {
      "criterion": "Every generated install command begins with an idempotent `git clone --depth 1 https://github.com/banodoco/Reigh-Worker.git \"<workerRepoPath>\"`, followed by the shell-appropriate cd (`cd` on Linux; `Set-Location -LiteralPath` on PowerShell; `cd /d` on cmd with the `/d` flag explicitly asserted in tests).",
      "priority": "must"
    },
    {
      "criterion": "Every generated RUN command begins with the shell-appropriate cd AND then a `.uv-migrated` sentinel preguard that renames stale `venv/` / `.venv/` to timestamped backups BEFORE `git pull` + `uv sync`. This is asserted in `commandUtils.test.ts` for Linux (`[ ! -f .uv-migrated ]`), PowerShell (`if (-not (Test-Path .uv-migrated))`), and cmd (`if not exist .uv-migrated`).",
      "priority": "must"
    },
    {
      "criterion": "Every install and run command selects `--extra cuda124` when `gpuType !== \"nvidia-50\"` and `--extra cuda128` when `gpuType === \"nvidia-50\"` \u2014 identical branching to today's `commandUtils.ts:42` torch install. Hard-coded `cuda124` is explicitly NOT present in the Windows code paths.",
      "priority": "must"
    },
    {
      "criterion": "`commandUtils.test.ts` asserts gpuType branching (cuda124 AND cuda128 cases) for Linux, Windows PowerShell, AND Windows cmd across both install and run, so a future regression dropping gpuType branching is caught at test time.",
      "priority": "must"
    },
    {
      "criterion": "`reigh-app/src/shared/components/SettingsModal/types.ts` is in Step 7's file list and `CommandConfig` there gains a required `workerRepoPath: string` field. The extension lives in types.ts, not in commandUtils.ts.",
      "priority": "must"
    },
    {
      "criterion": "`workerRepoPath` is added as a new persisted field on `WorkerLaunchConfigValues` and `WorkerLaunchConfigSetters` in `useWorkerLaunchConfig.ts` (7th `usePersistentState` call), with defaults `%USERPROFILE%\\Reigh-Worker` (Windows) and `$HOME/reigh-worker` (Linux), and threaded through `sections/GenerationSection/GenerationSection.tsx` into `GenerationTokenPanel.tsx` into `CommandConfig`. `SettingsModal.tsx` is NOT modified.",
      "priority": "must"
    },
    {
      "criterion": "`GenerationTokenPanel.tsx` exposes a 'Worker repo location' text input bound to `launchConfig.workerRepoPath` / `launchSetters.setWorkerRepoPath`.",
      "priority": "must"
    },
    {
      "criterion": "The Linux install command contains an inline `apt-cache show python3.10-venv` precheck so Ubuntu 24.04+ users hit an actionable error.",
      "priority": "must"
    },
    {
      "criterion": "Phase 3 validation includes THREE gating smoke tests: (a) wrong-cwd, (b) fresh-machine, (c) run-path-migration from existing `venv/` with no `.uv-migrated` sentinel \u2014 for all three shells. Any failure blocks the migration.",
      "priority": "must"
    },
    {
      "criterion": "The orchestrator RunPod startup template launches via `uv run --python 3.10 python worker.py ...` (unchanged CLI args) after `uv sync --locked --python 3.10 --extra cuda124` succeeds.",
      "priority": "must"
    },
    {
      "criterion": "Step 7 begins with a grep audit verifying `GenerationTokenPanel.tsx` is the only non-test caller of the helpers; only then does the mac contract switch to `throw UnsupportedPlatformError`.",
      "priority": "must"
    },
    {
      "criterion": "The implementation preserves existing `venv/` or `.venv/` directories during the first uv-managed migration via timestamped backups and writes a `.uv-migrated` sentinel only after sync succeeds, triggered by either the Install or Run command path.",
      "priority": "must"
    },
    {
      "criterion": "`test_startup_script.py` asserts the new phase-sequence, the strict retry-then-exit with `%{http_code}` case match, and the lenient path for subsequent phases.",
      "priority": "must"
    },
    {
      "criterion": "Plan Overview prose matches the detailed step contracts: (a) strict helper uses `%{http_code}` NOT `--fail`; (b) orchestrator control-loop policy is 'no unconditional changes; one conditional cap bump gated on Step 6 benchmark' \u2014 not 'no orchestrator control-loop changes'.",
      "priority": "must"
    },
    {
      "criterion": "`reigh-worker/STRUCTURE.md` documents the Wan2GP subtree drift risk as accepted debt.",
      "priority": "should"
    },
    {
      "criterion": "README.md and `GenerationTokenPanel.tsx` both name Ubuntu 22.04 LTS / Debian 12 as the supported Linux baseline and point Ubuntu 24.04+ users at deadsnakes PPA.",
      "priority": "should"
    },
    {
      "criterion": "The Overview names `uv python install 3.10` as considered-and-rejected with a reason.",
      "priority": "should"
    },
    {
      "criterion": "The Overview documents the mac-throw maintenance hazard as accepted tech debt with a pointer toward `NonMacComputerType`.",
      "priority": "should"
    },
    {
      "criterion": "The active orchestrator path has a single source of startup-script truth; `runpod_client.py` remains only as an unreferenced compatibility shim.",
      "priority": "should"
    },
    {
      "criterion": "Reigh-owned docs/debug helpers are updated in the same rollout including both copies of `task_worker_lifecycle.md`.",
      "priority": "should"
    },
    {
      "criterion": "Manual smoke tests pass for Linux local, Windows PowerShell cuda124, Windows PowerShell cuda128, Windows cmd cuda124, Windows cmd cuda128, RunPod upgrade, macOS guardrail, fresh-machine bootstrap, wrong-cwd bootstrap, and run-path migration; failure-path tests cover missing `uv`, offline sync, corrupted lockfile, first-PATCH Supabase 5xx, and Ubuntu 24.04 without deadsnakes.",
      "priority": "info"
    }
  ],
  "assumptions": [
    "RunPod's runtime interpreter is Python 3.10 (verified against runpod_client.py:357/985/993 and worker_startup.template.sh:105/113).",
    "`worker_state.py:140` reads `startup_phase` from an in-memory polled dict; verified no pod-file reader exists in gpu_orchestrator/.",
    "`worker_state.py:200-204`'s 30-minute startup safety net catches pods that exit before signaling any phase. Conditional bump to 45 min is gated on measured cold-sync p95 from Step 6.",
    "`CommandConfig` is defined in `reigh-app/src/shared/components/SettingsModal/types.ts:13` with `gpuType: string` at line 15 \u2014 verified. `commandUtils.ts:42` already branches on `gpuType === \"nvidia-50\"` to select the cuda128 torch install, and the new `uv sync --extra` must preserve this branch.",
    "Only Windows exposes the 50-series GPU option today; Linux and RunPod stay on cuda124. If that changes, the plan's `--extra` branching generalizes cleanly because it is keyed on gpuType, not computerType.",
    "The persistence seam for worker-launch settings is `useWorkerLaunchConfig.ts`; `SettingsModal.tsx` only consumes the hook and is NOT modified.",
    "`GenerationSection.tsx` forwards `launchConfig`/`launchSetters` to `GenerationTokenPanel`; TypeScript forces propagation once `WorkerLaunchConfigValues` grows `workerRepoPath`.",
    "Current `commandUtils.ts:53,66` install commands begin with `git clone --depth 1 https://github.com/banodoco/Reigh-Worker.git` \u2014 preserved (with idempotency preguard) in the new shape.",
    "Idempotent clone preguards and sentinel preguards are shell-appropriate: `[ ... ]` on Linux, `Test-Path` on PowerShell, `if not exist` on cmd. Re-running is a no-op for already-migrated users.",
    "`curl --fail` has inconsistent cross-version behavior on error bodies, so the plan uses explicit `--write-out '%{http_code}'` + case match.",
    "macOS remains unsupported; `GenerationTokenPanel.tsx` is the only non-test caller. `NonMacComputerType` narrow type is accepted debt.",
    "The orchestrator worker image stays on CUDA 12.4 (`cuda124` extra); the client keeps its existing GPU-specific branching and the plan preserves that branching in every generated command shape.",
    "The web client cannot bundle binaries; `uv` bootstrap happens via the official Astral installer.",
    "Keeping `requirements.txt` committed during rollout is acceptable as rollback ballast.",
    "`uv python install 3.10` is intentionally NOT adopted in this rollout.",
    "Folding `Wan2GP/requirements.txt` into Reigh's `pyproject.toml` creates subtree drift risk; accepted debt.",
    "Keeping `python worker.py` as the orchestrator launch entrypoint is behaviorally equivalent to today.",
    "Linux local baseline is Ubuntu 22.04 LTS / Debian 12; 24.04+ users need deadsnakes PPA, surfaced via inline `apt-cache` precheck.",
    "Run users who copy-paste the Run command instead of the Install command must still be migrated correctly. The sentinel preguard in every Run shape is the mechanism; Phase 3 smoke test (c) is the proof."
  ],

  "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-007",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: for ordinary pairs, the new diagnosis is not well-supported by the repo. the current sync effect in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l62) already syncs `url` and `primaryvariantid`, and the regenerate path reads exactly `starturl`, `endurl`, `startgenid`, `endgenid`, `startvariantid`, and `endvariantid` from `segmentslotmode.pairdata` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts#l438). adding `thumburl` and `generationid` to the sync does not, by itself, explain why an old image url is still being used for regular pairs.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-008",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the fix is incomplete for extra trailing slots. `handlepairclick` can populate `activepairdata` from `trailingpairdata` when `pairindex === pairdatabyindex.size` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l121), but the proposed sync effect only reads `pairdatabyindex.get(segmentslotlightboxindex)`. for those trailing-slot cases `fresh` is `undefined`, so no image refresh happens.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-018",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the merge pseudocode does not actually sync 'all image fields' as claimed. it omits `id` and `position` from change detection, and when `fresh.startimage` or `fresh.endimage` becomes `null` it preserves the stale previous object instead of syncing that null state. as written, it also needs explicit null-guards around spreads of `prev.startimage` / `prev.endimage` to be safe in strict typescript.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-106",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan's claim that `worker_state.py:200-204` (the 30-minute startup safety net) catches abandoned pods that exited before patching is slightly imprecise. line 200-204 only runs when `in_startup_phase` is true, which requires `startup_phase` to be in `('deps_installing', 'deps_verified', 'worker_starting')` per line 161. a pod that exits before its first patch has `startup_phase = none`, so `in_startup_phase` is false and the code falls through to the normal timeout checks (active_stale / not_claiming) at line 207+, not the 30-minute startup cap. both paths eventually reap the worker, so the end result is the same, but the overview's reasoning (naming 200-204 specifically) is wrong. worth correcting so future readers don't mis-reason about the invariant.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-107",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked worker_state.py line 161 (`in_startup_phase = startup_phase in (...)`) against the plan's assumption that subsequent lenient writes are safe. if deps_installing succeeds but deps_verified silently fails, startup_phase stays at 'deps_installing' and the 30-minute cap from line 200-204 applies \u2014 even though the pod is actually progressing through `uv sync`. on a slow runpod image with cold wheel downloads, `uv sync --extra cuda124` plus wan2gp deps can take 20+ minutes. there's no concrete benchmark in the plan that says the 30-minute cap is safely above the expected sync time; if `uv sync` legitimately takes 25 minutes and the deps_verified patch fails on a transient network blip, the orchestrator will terminate a healthy pod. plan should either bump the cap or note that deps_verified swallowing is acceptable because the cap is generously above typical sync time.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    }
  ],

      "concern": "dependency resolution: the plan does not lock down the active python-version matrix even though the repo currently splits between python 3.10 local installs and a python 3.11 runpod image.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-013",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the package is internally inconsistent. the plan body says to keep `activepairdata` in `usestate` and make phase 2 optional, but the attached metadata and success criteria still describe the previous derive-via-`usememo` / remove-`setactivepairdata` / simplify-`onpairclick` plan. that means the approved-plan requirements are only partially coherent as presented.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-108",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the linux distro note is documentation-only; the generated install command in step 7 still runs `apt-get install python3.10-venv python3.10-dev ffmpeg` without any pre-check that the package exists. users on ubuntu 24.04+ who ignore the doc and try to copy-paste the command will hit a confusing `e: unable to locate package python3.10-venv` from apt rather than a targeted error from commandutils.ts. a one-line detection pre-check (e.g., `apt-cache show python3.10-venv >/dev/null 2>&1 || { echo 'install deadsnakes ppa first \u2014 see readme'; exit 1; }`) would convert the silent failure into an actionable error, but the plan does not add this.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-014",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: phase 1 still misses a real supporting-data location: `trailingpairdata` lives outside `pairdatabyindex` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotpresentationadapter.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotpresentationadapter.ts#l111), but the proposed sync logic only considers `pairdatabyindex`. if trailing slot regenerate is part of the bug surface, that location is not covered.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-110",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the non-swallowing helper `update_worker_phase_strict` is a new shell function, but phase 1 step 2 introduces it only as pseudo-code. there is no concrete note that the helper must handle supabase http non-2xx responses as failures (curl returns 0 even on http 4xx/5xx by default unless `-f` or `--fail-with-body` is used). if the implementer ports `update_worker_phase` verbatim with only `|| return 0` removed, a 500 from supabase would still report success because curl's exit code is 0. the plan should specify `curl --fail` or explicit http status code checking in the strict variant.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-109",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the step 2 retry loop uses a naked `sleep 2` between attempts, which adds up to 6 seconds of latency on the happy path when the first patch eventually succeeds on attempt 2 or 3. for pods where supabase is reachable but slow during the initial moments of a cold runpod start, the retry logic is fine. however, if the first attempt fails with a tls handshake delay and the retry loop sleeps 2s per attempt regardless of whether curl itself has been blocking for tens of seconds, the total startup latency penalty could be substantial. this is a minor tuning concern, not a correctness issue.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "hook-abstraction": [

      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    }
  ],

  {
    "subsystem": "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct",
    "total_occurrences": 5,
    "plan_count": 2,
    "entries": [
      {
        "id": "DEBT-007",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: for ordinary pairs, the new diagnosis is not well-supported by the repo. the current sync effect in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l62) already syncs `url` and `primaryvariantid`, and the regenerate path reads exactly `starturl`, `endurl`, `startgenid`, `endgenid`, `startvariantid`, and `endvariantid` from `segmentslotmode.pairdata` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts#l438). adding `thumburl` and `generationid` to the sync does not, by itself, explain why an old image url is still being used for regular pairs.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-008",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the fix is incomplete for extra trailing slots. `handlepairclick` can populate `activepairdata` from `trailingpairdata` when `pairindex === pairdatabyindex.size` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l121), but the proposed sync effect only reads `pairdatabyindex.get(segmentslotlightboxindex)`. for those trailing-slot cases `fresh` is `undefined`, so no image refresh happens.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-018",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the merge pseudocode does not actually sync 'all image fields' as claimed. it omits `id` and `position` from change detection, and when `fresh.startimage` or `fresh.endimage` becomes `null` it preserves the stale previous object instead of syncing that null state. as written, it also needs explicit null-guards around spreads of `prev.startimage` / `prev.endimage` to be safe in strict typescript.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-106",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan's claim that `worker_state.py:200-204` (the 30-minute startup safety net) catches abandoned pods that exited before patching is slightly imprecise. line 200-204 only runs when `in_startup_phase` is true, which requires `startup_phase` to be in `('deps_installing', 'deps_verified', 'worker_starting')` per line 161. a pod that exits before its first patch has `startup_phase = none`, so `in_startup_phase` is false and the code falls through to the normal timeout checks (active_stale / not_claiming) at line 207+, not the 30-minute startup cap. both paths eventually reap the worker, so the end result is the same, but the overview's reasoning (naming 200-204 specifically) is wrong. worth correcting so future readers don't mis-reason about the invariant.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-107",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked worker_state.py line 161 (`in_startup_phase = startup_phase in (...)`) against the plan's assumption that subsequent lenient writes are safe. if deps_installing succeeds but deps_verified silently fails, startup_phase stays at 'deps_installing' and the 30-minute cap from line 200-204 applies \u2014 even though the pod is actually progressing through `uv sync`. on a slow runpod image with cold wheel downloads, `uv sync --extra cuda124` plus wan2gp deps can take 20+ minutes. there's no concrete benchmark in the plan that says the 30-minute cap is safely above the expected sync time; if `uv sync` legitimately takes 25 minutes and the deps_verified patch fails on a transient network blip, the orchestrator will terminate a healthy pod. plan should either bump the cap or note that deps_verified swallowing is acceptable because the cap is generously above typical sync time.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
        ]
      }
    ]
  },
